# SL3 — Claude_Task_Plan.md history archive

**HISTORY ONLY — NOT INSTRUCTIONS.** Nothing in this file binds any routine, and no routine needs to read it to do its job. Each entry is the full original text of a `Claude_Task_Plan.md` paragraph as it stood on 2026-10-30, kept for its incident evidence and reasoning; the plan now carries a condensed, rules-only version that points here. Where the two differ, the plan wins. Superseded, retired or since-corrected instructions appear here exactly as they were written; never execute them from this file.

## H-SL3-01 — DEPENDENCY GATE — SEPARATE and FATAL, do NOT wrap — CALL ops.sp_assert_deps('SL3', ['D2a'], <today) BEFORE the

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (SL3, was line 5277) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

**DEPENDENCY GATE — SEPARATE and FATAL, do NOT wrap — `CALL ops.sp_assert_deps('SL3', ['D2a'], <today>)` BEFORE the start-log.** Aborts (RAISE) + `missing_dependency` critical if D2a has not logged `completed` for today (SL3 measures against D2a's freshly-ingested marks). Self-bootstrapping.
   - **UPSTREAM-HALT LIMB — WHAT THIS GATE DOES WHEN D2a COULD NOT RUN AT ALL, AND WHY SL3'S DISPOSITION IS NOT AR_orc'S (added 2026-09-21 by SL3's own fire, which took this branch live: the 4th BigQuery OAuth grant expiry halted the D2a slot at 16:40 MT having written NO `ops.run_log` row at all — halt record commit `3d5716c`, `ops/spikes/bigquery-deauth-2026-09-21-d2a-halt-record.md`).** A connector-outage halt writes no `ops.run_log` row **by construction** — the log sink and the alert sink are both the connector that is down — so the shared DEPENDENCY-WAIT WINDOW's branch list does not fit this case: its FUTILE branch keys on an upstream `'failed'`/`'halted'` row TODAY, which such an upstream can never have written, so **the halt class that is most certainly futile is the only one that reads as "NO upstream row today" and buys the full window.** D2a is `catchup_safe: false`, so that branch's ACTIVE REPAIR arm is unavailable too and the literal reading lands on "otherwise plain 60-min window" — a full futile poll against an upstream whose death is already established on `origin/main` (halved to ~30 min by the open-chain clamp, four `missing_dependency` rows being open tonight, but halving a futile wait does not make it evidence-driven). **THE RULE, and it is deliberately the same one AR_orc landed hours earlier for its own gate rather than a second invention: a same-day halt record for D2a on `origin/main` establishes the FUTILE branch's condition — take it, exhaust immediately, emit `DEPWAIT[dep=D2a;polls=<n>;waited_s=<total>;outcome=futile;refired=none]`, and NEVER convert that halt record into a `completed` row.** The machinery already protects that last clause and it is worth knowing why rather than trusting it: `sp_assert_deps` calls `ops.sp_backfill_run_log_from_markers()` on entry, and RUNBOOK §48 keeps halt records OUT of `ops.routine_commit_markers` precisely so the backfill cannot manufacture the completion — verified tonight, today's markers are `D1` and `SL2` only, and the gate correctly re-RAISEd on the poll.
     - **AND THE HALF THAT IS SL3'S ALONE, WHICH AR_orc'S LIMB CANNOT CARRY: SL3'S MISSED EVENING IS RECOVERABLE, AND SL3 CORRECTLY NEEDS NO PLACE IN THE REPLAY ORDER.** AR_orc and SL5 are both `catchup_safe: false` and their missed drain is simply lost, so their limbs select a wait branch and stop there. SL3 is `catchup_safe: true` **and** owns STEP 1's multi-day catch-up loop, so its next scheduled fire re-covers the skipped evening by itself — **conditional on exactly one thing, which is the fact worth stating HERE rather than leaving behind the gate that just stopped the run: STEP 1's loop scopes its day set to days carrying a `state.daily_marks_curated` row, so the skipped day returns only once D2a's OWN marks catch-up lands it.** That is emphatically NOT OPS0 refiring D2a — it never will, D2a being `catchup_safe: false` — and it is MEASURED, not assumed: all four of the most recent Fridays (2026-08-28, 2026-09-04, 2026-09-11, 2026-09-18) carry a `state.daily_marks_curated` row with NO same-day D2a completion, D2a's Sun-Thu cadence leaving each Friday's marks to the following Sunday's fire. **So both recovery plans written during tonight's outage are RIGHT to omit SL3, and incomplete only in saying nothing about why:** D2a's halt record §6 step 3 orders the replay as "D2a for 2026-09-21, then D2, then D3" and its step 5 names the expected `missed_run` + staleness signature as "D1/D2/D2a/D3", while the string `SL3` appears NOWHERE in that file — yet SL3 and D2 are the ONLY two routines in the 33-routine fleet declaring D2a as a dependency (`ops/cadence.yaml`, measured this run). SL3's omission is correct BECAUSE of the catch-up loop and for no other reason, so a triager meeting SL3's `halted` row and its `missing_dependency` critical outside that signature list should read them as this limb's EXPECTED outcome and not as a fresh fault. That gap is recorded as an `ops.alerts` `info` row naming the owning surface and deliberately NOT repaired from here — a spike file is another slot's record, and SL3 edits no other routine's surface by design. **ADJUDICATED AND FIXED BY W5 ON ITS 2026-10-11 FIRE — `da0310fb` reads `resolved=TRUE` at 23:24:04 MT, repair in commit `b80eead8` (recorded 2026-10-12, SL3 re-resolve; both sites re-read against the tree, not taken from the `resolved_note`).** The shared Observability §WHAT EVERY HALTED SLOT OWES now carries item 6 — a halt record's recovery procedure names EVERY `depends_on` dependent of the halted routine, a `catchup_safe` one listed as needing no manual replay with that reason stated — and the 2026-09-21 D2a halt record gained a dated ADDENDUM after its §6 step 5 naming SL3 (expected signature read as D1/D2/D2a/D3/SL3, no manual replay). The original steps were left as written, so the record's step 3/5 text still omits SL3 and the addendum is what governs; nothing in this limb's own disposition changes.

## H-SL3-02 — STEP 1 — UPDATE THE FORWARDTEST SERIES. Catchup loop (owner directive 20260725 CATCHUP EVIDENCE WINDOW — see O

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (SL3, was line 5281) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

STEP 1 — UPDATE THE FORWARD-TEST SERIES. **Catch-up loop (owner directive 2026-07-25 CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW — see Observability § above). THE NESTING IS MEMBER-OUTER / DAY-INNER AND THE DAY SET IS PER MEMBER — corrected 2026-09-24 (SL3), because this sentence described ONE SHARED day set and the 2026-09-23 per-member bound had already made that quantity impossible to compute; do NOT reduce the per-member bounds to a single date, `MAX` re-opening the 2026-09-23 grain defect and `MIN` turning the forward test into a backtest (see the nesting bullet below).** For EVERY SHADOW/PAPER strategy in `state.strategy_roster`, derive THAT MEMBER'S OWN day set — every trading day `>= COALESCE(<`MAX(incubation_day)` in `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` FOR THAT `strategy_code`>, <that member's own SHADOW-entry `events.strategy_lifecycle` row date>)`, read INCLUSIVELY (see the bound bullets below), and `<= last_trading_day`, that has a `state.daily_marks_curated` row — and run the bullets below against THAT day's marks, oldest first, not only today's (this backfill depends on D2a's own marks catch-up — see D2a — a day D2a never marked cannot be evaluated here). **The shared CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW read is still owed and is a DIFFERENT quantity: resolve `state.routine_catchup_window` for routine='SL3' (or `SELECT MAX(run_date) FROM ops.run_log WHERE routine='SL3' AND status='completed'` as fallback) for the window-widening check and the `CATCHUP[window_days=<N>]` token — that window is per ROUTINE, the day set is per MEMBER, and since 2026-09-22 the routine window is no longer the day-set bound.** For each such member, and for each day in its own day set, oldest first:
   - SHADOW: compute router activation + entry/exit SIGNALS against fresh `events.daily_marks` (zero orders, zero capital); record would-be-trade regime cell + signal-generation rate vs declared frequency + scaffolding correctness to `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` (`phase='shadow'`) and record each would-be signal as an `events.shadow_positions` row (`event_type='SIGNAL'`, `is_paper=TRUE`, `regime_cell`, `note` = one-line signal rationale). The `regime_cell` value MUST use the two-token coverage key `SPY_TREND/VIX_REGIME` (for example `NEUTRAL/NORMAL`), with both tokens drawn from `strategy/01_shared_regime_vocabulary.md`; yield-curve and breadth remain separate signal context. This is the exact key used by `state.arsenal_regime_coverage`.
   - PAPER: compute SIMULATED fills at live marks with modeled IBKR commissions + conservative slippage vs SGOV; update `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` (`phase='paper'`: sim_twr, sgov_twr, excess, peak_to_trough, sim_closed_trades, per-regime-cell attribution) and record each simulated fill as `events.shadow_positions` rows (`event_type='OPEN'`/`'CLOSE'`, `shares`/`price`/`commission` = the modeled values, `is_paper=TRUE`). Zero real orders — `events.shadow_positions` (is_paper=TRUE) is excluded from every live capital/kill view. IDEMPOTENCY GUARD (required — OPS0 catchup autofire can re-invoke SL3 same-day, and now also a genuine multi-day catch-up loop covering more than one trading day in one session): before each insert, NOT-EXISTS check on (strategy_code, event_type, ticker, DATE(event_ts) = <the trading day currently being processed, today included>) — skip already-recorded rows, mirroring the `ops.roster_change_log` NOT-EXISTS pattern.
   - **`analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` WRITE CONTRACT — PINNED (SL3 2026-08-19 diligence sweep, handoff-wiring class); binds BOTH bullets above.** That table has exactly three `NOT NULL`-with-no-default columns — `strategy_code`, `phase`, and **`incubation_day` (DATE)** — and until this line, `incubation_day` appeared **nowhere in this document at all**, while SL3 is its ONLY writer. Same shape as the SL3→SL5 `PENDING_ROSTER` lane (fixed 6467b4d) and the SL5 `change_key` strings (pinned 2026-08-19): a required column the write site's own text never names. Two things are pinned, and the second matters more than the first:
     - **`incubation_day` = the trading day currently being processed by the catch-up loop above — NEVER `CURRENT_DATE()`.** Every gate in STEP 2 is computed off it and a session that stamped today's date onto a backfilled day would corrupt all of them silently: `shadow_days` and `paper_days` are `COUNT(DISTINCT incubation_day)` (`bigquery/266`'s `shadow_days` alias inside its `agg` CTE — the live SHADOW definition since 2026-10-25, repinned that day by SL3 from `bigquery/60`:34, which a SUPERSEDED header line had shifted to :35; `bigquery/35`'s `paper_days` alias), so a multi-day catch-up run would collapse N days into ONE and push the 20-day SHADOW and 60-day PAPER floors permanently out of reach; and `excess_met` reads `ARRAY_AGG(excess ORDER BY incubation_day DESC LIMIT 10)` (`bigquery/35`'s `trailing_excess` CTE, read by its `excess_met` alias), so a wrong stamp reorders the trailing-10-day sustained-excess window at the highest-stakes PAPER→PROBE gate. It is also the table's `PARTITION BY` key (`bigquery/35`'s `PARTITION BY incubation_day CLUSTER BY strategy_code, phase` clause), so a `CURRENT_DATE()` stamp mis-partitions the row as well as mis-dating it. The full column set: `strategy_code`, `phase` (`'shadow'`/`'paper'`), `incubation_day`, plus the phase's own measures (`signals_generated`/`scaffolding_faults` for shadow; `sim_twr`/`sgov_twr`/`excess`/`peak_to_trough`/`sim_closed_trades` for paper) and `regime_cell`; `computed_ts` defaults.
     - **CITATION FORM — audited 2026-08-23, do NOT re-add a `:NNN` pin into `bigquery/35`.** All 19 `path:NNN` citations in this section were resolved against the tree: 12 accurate, 6 drifted, 1 (the `bigquery/133` F/G claim, corrected in STEP 4) an overclaim rather than a stale number. **Every drift was in `bigquery/35_strategy_arsenal.sql` — 6 of its 7 pins — because that file keeps accreting long inline notes above the constructs cited here**, while the pins into `bigquery/52`, `bigquery/60` and `bigquery/01_schema.sql` were accurate without exception. The `bigquery/35` citations are therefore now named anchors (`paper_days` alias, the `trailing_excess` column alias inside the `agg` CTE, the `PARTITION BY` clause, `not_already_transitioned`, the `cooldown_until DATE` column, the `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW`/`CREATE TABLE` statements) — **two of those six sharpened 2026-08-24 (SL3) after a re-audit resolved every anchor against the tree: `trailing_excess` is a column alias, NOT a CTE of its own (it lives inside the `agg` CTE), and `not_already_transitioned` is the ONE anchor here that is not self-disambiguating — a bare grep for it hits THREE different readiness views in `bigquery/35` (the `UNDER_REVIEW->SHADOW` adoption one, the `SHADOW->PAPER` shadow one, and the `PAPER->PROBE` paper one), so always grep it together with the `change_key` literal named alongside it in the prose, never by the alias name alone.** An anchor that resolves to three places is the same hazard as a drifted line number in a slower form, all grep-resolvable; the pins into the three stable files are left as measured-correct line numbers rather than churned. **A drifted pin is worse than no pin — it points at real code that is not the code meant.** **RE-AUDITED IN FULL 2026-09-10 (SL3), every citation in this block resolved against the tree: 14 remain (down from 19, as pins were converted to anchors), of which 11 are accurate to the exact line and 3 were not** — `ops/handoff_contracts.yaml`:124-125 (drifted to 141-142; converted to a named anchor, see STEP 5), `state.strategy_shadow_readiness`:516 (never resolvable, and a claim about SL5's prose that SL5 had already repaired; withdrawn, see STEP 3) and `bigquery/60`:64, which is **re-measured ADEQUATE and must NOT be churned**: it is cited for `signal_rate_ok` carrying an archetype-aware upper ceiling, the ceiling expression spans lines 61-64, and line 64 is the `) AS signal_rate_ok,` alias that names the very column the citation is about. A pin into the closing line of the construct it names is not drift, and re-numbering it to 63 would trade a correct anchor for a more fragile one. **The pattern across all three is the reusable part and it sharpens the 08-23 conclusion rather than repeating it:** that pass concluded every drift was in `bigquery/35` "because that file keeps accreting long inline notes", and all three found today are consistent with the same mechanism applied to files 08-23 did not think to include — a YAML spec whose header comment grows with every allowlist entry, and a prose document that is nothing but accreting notes. **The rule is therefore about the TARGET's growth rate, not about `bigquery/35`: line-pin only files that do not accrete above the cited construct, and never a prose document at all.**
     - **THE IDEMPOTENCY GUARD ABOVE MUST COVER THIS TABLE TOO — NOT-EXISTS on `(strategy_code, phase, incubation_day)` before each insert.** **AND THE SPELLING IS PINNED, BECAUSE THE OBVIOUS ONE DOES NOT COMPILE (added 2026-09-23, SL3; reproduced against BigQuery, not inferred).** Both guards in this STEP — this one and the `events.shadow_positions` one above — are stated as prose ("NOT-EXISTS check on (…) before each insert") with no executable template anywhere, and the form a session naturally reaches for, `INSERT … SELECT <literals> WHERE NOT EXISTS (…)`, fails at analysis time: **`Query without FROM clause cannot have a WHERE clause`**. A `SELECT` of bare literals has no `FROM`, so it cannot carry the `WHERE` the guard lives in. **Write it with a `FROM UNNEST([…])` so the SELECT has a FROM clause** — `INSERT … SELECT <cols> FROM UNNEST([1]) WHERE NOT EXISTS (…)` — or read first and branch in the session; both compile. The failure is LOUD and pre-write, so nothing can be silently duplicated by it, which is why this is a spelling pin and not a defect record. It is nonetheless the 2026-09-16 blind spot arriving on the GUARD rather than on the write it guards — "the instruction that executes most often is the one least likely to carry a template, precisely because nothing ever broke" — and these two guards are the only writes in this block with no template at all. As written, that guard named only `events.shadow_positions`, leaving the perf table with no re-run protection even though the same two re-invocation paths (an OPS0 catch-up autofire, and the multi-day catch-up loop) hit both. This is NOT harmless duplication that the DISTINCT day-counts absorb: `state.strategy_shadow_readiness` **SUMs** `signals_generated` and `scaffolding_faults` across rows (`bigquery/266`'s `agg` CTE `SUM(signals_generated)`/`SUM(scaffolding_faults)` — repinned 2026-10-25 by SL3 from `bigquery/60`:35-36, superseded by `bigquery/266` and shifted one line), and since `bigquery/52_shadow_readiness_band.sql` gave `signal_rate_ok` an archetype-aware upper CEILING rather than a bare floor (`bigquery/52`:70, repinned from :69 the same day; live at `bigquery/266`'s `signal_rate_ok` alias), a duplicated day inflates the signal count toward that ceiling — so a re-run can push a genuinely healthy candidate to `signal_rate_ok=FALSE`, and, once `shadow_days >= 400`, straight into STEP 4's SHADOW time-cull as `stuck`. The failure direction is a **false REJECT of a sound candidate**, which no other rail catches.
   - **`events.shadow_positions` WRITE CONTRACT — `event_ts` MUST BE STAMPED TO THE TRADING DAY BEING PROCESSED, NEVER LEFT TO DEFAULT (SL3 2026-08-26 diligence sweep; the sibling half of the `incubation_day` pin above, left open when that one was closed).** `events.shadow_positions` has exactly one `NOT NULL`-with-no-default column (`strategy_code`), so the required-column audit that produced the pin above comes back clean here — and that is precisely why this defect survived it. The exposure is not a missing required column; it is a **defaulted** one whose default is wrong for this writer. `event_ts` is `TIMESTAMP DEFAULT CURRENT_TIMESTAMP()` and is also the table's partition key (`bigquery/35_strategy_arsenal.sql`'s `CREATE TABLE … events.shadow_positions` block and its `PARTITION BY DATE(event_ts) CLUSTER BY strategy_code` clause), while SL3's block names `event_ts` exactly twice — at STEP 3 and STEP 4, both times to say it defaults, and both times about `events.strategy_lifecycle`, where defaulting is CORRECT because a lifecycle transition genuinely happens at write time. **A `shadow_positions` row does not: it records a signal or a simulated fill that happened on a specific trading day, which during a catch-up backfill is NOT today.** Neither STEP 1 bullet tells the session to supply `event_ts`, so as written every backfilled row lands stamped with the RUN date.
     - **This silently defeats STEP 1's own idempotency guard, in BOTH directions.** The guard keys on `DATE(event_ts) = <the trading day currently being processed>` — a column that, unstamped, carries the run date instead. So (a) for every backfilled day X ≠ today the guard is **vacuous**: no row ever carries `DATE(event_ts) = X`, so a second session covering the same catch-up window re-inserts every one of them — the exact re-run exposure the guard exists to close; and (b) worse, within a SINGLE multi-day catch-up session processing oldest-first, every row already written for days X₁…Xₙ₋₁ carries `DATE(event_ts) = today`, so when the loop reaches today the guard's NOT-EXISTS **matches those backfill rows** for any coinciding `(strategy_code, event_type, ticker)` and **skips today's genuine signal as "already recorded"**. A systematic strategy re-firing on the same ticker across consecutive days is the NORMAL case, not an exotic one, so that collision is likely rather than remote. Direction (b) is a silent LOSS of live signal evidence, which is strictly worse than the duplication in (a) and is the same failure direction — a false negative on the candidate's own record — that the `incubation_day` pin above calls out as the one no other rail catches.
     - **Mis-partitioning is the third harm**, identical to the one the pin above states for `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf`: `PARTITION BY DATE(event_ts)` files a backfilled day's rows in today's partition. **Stamp `event_ts` explicitly on every `shadow_positions` insert** — the same trading day the loop is processing and the same value used for that row's `incubation_day`; the two tables are written from one loop iteration and must agree on what day it is. `events.shadow_positions` holds **0 rows** (verified live 2026-08-26), so — like the `SHADOW->PAPER` marker (2026-08-19) and the `->REJECTED` marker (2026-08-20) — this is closed before its first firing with no historical row to remap. SL3 is the table's only writer anywhere in the repo (repo-wide grep, 2026-08-26: `bigquery/35`'s DDL, `ops/handoff_contracts.yaml`'s registration of it as SL3's write surface, `ops/cadence.yaml`'s scope list, and SL3's own text — no other write site), so no other routine's text needs the same pin.
   - **`regime_cell` MUST BE RESOLVED AS-OF THE TRADING DAY BEING PROCESSED, AND ITS SOURCE IS `events.regime_events` — NOT `state.current_regime` (SL3 2026-08-27 diligence sweep; measured live, not inferred).** This is the THIRD as-of-dated field in these same two write sites and the last one still unpinned. The two already closed have the identical shape — a per-day value that a catch-up backfill silently fills with the RUN day's value: `incubation_day` (2026-08-19) and `events.shadow_positions.event_ts` (2026-08-26, the bullet directly above). `regime_cell` was never pinned because the STEP 1 SHADOW bullet pins its FORMAT and stops there: it requires the two-token `SPY_TREND/VIX_REGIME` key with both tokens drawn from `strategy/01_shared_regime_vocabulary.md`, and says nothing whatever about the field's SOURCE or its AS-OF DATE. **This section names `regime_cell` four times and `state.arsenal_regime_coverage` five, and names no source object for it even once**; the Read access scope line above granted none either (corrected in this same revision).
     - **The source is `events.regime_events`, read at `as_of_date = <the trading day currently being processed>`.** D2a — SL3's own declared dependency, so the dependency gate already guarantees the row is written before SL3 runs — writes one `scope='TECHNICAL_SIGNAL'` row per trading day for each of `key='SPY_TREND'` and `key='VIX_REGIME'`, carrying `as_of_date` and the exact vocabulary tokens, into a table that is `PARTITION BY as_of_date CLUSTER BY scope, key` (`bigquery/01_schema.sql`'s `CREATE TABLE … events.regime_events` block). Compose the cell as `CONCAT(<SPY_TREND value>, '/', <VIX_REGIME value>)` — which is exactly the key `state.arsenal_regime_coverage`'s own `cells` CTE builds by `CROSS JOIN`ing `['UP','NEUTRAL','DOWN']` against `['LOW','NORMAL','HIGH']`. Where a day carries more than one row for a key, take the latest by `(event_ts DESC, event_id DESC)` — the same tiebreaker `state.current_regime`'s `QUALIFY` uses, and for the reason its own inline comment gives (two events can share `(as_of_date, event_ts)` for one `(scope, key)`, as STRATEGY_ACTIVATION C did on 2026-06-03, so without the `event_id` term the resolved cell can flip between query runs).
     - **Do NOT reach for `state.current_regime`, which is the trap this pin exists to close.** It is the obvious object — it is the only `state` object with "regime" in its name that carries a cell at all, and `state.arsenal_regime_coverage` is the coverage GRID rather than a daily series — but its body is `QUALIFY ROW_NUMBER() OVER (PARTITION BY scope, key ORDER BY as_of_date DESC, event_ts DESC, event_id DESC) = 1` (`bigquery/01_schema.sql`'s `CREATE OR REPLACE VIEW … state.current_regime` statement). It partitions on `(scope, key)` ONLY and returns exactly one row per key — the latest-dated one. **It has no as-of parameter and cannot be given one**, so a backfill that reads it stamps TODAY's regime onto every day it processes, silently and with no error.
     - **Live and measured, not a theoretical hazard — the cell genuinely moves day to day.** `VIX_REGIME` read `NORMAL` on 2026-08-25 and 2026-08-26 and flipped to `LOW` on 2026-08-27 (close 14.51 < 15), and did the same a week earlier: `LOW` on 08-19 sandwiched between `NORMAL` on 08-18 and 08-20. So on the very run that wrote this pin, a backfill of 2026-08-26 reading `state.current_regime` would have stamped `UP/LOW` onto a day that was `UP/NORMAL`. All nine trading days 2026-08-17..08-27 carry a complete `SPY_TREND`/`VIX_REGIME` pair with no duplicates (verified live 2026-08-27), so a wrong cell would be a choice of the wrong OBJECT, never missing data.
     - **The harm is worse than a mis-dated row, and it lands at the gate that spends money.** `regime_cell` is the column `state.arsenal_regime_coverage`'s `demonstrated` CTE groups by (`GROUP BY p.strategy_code, p.regime_cell` over `phase='paper'` rows), so a wrong cell both manufactures coverage evidence in a cell the candidate never traded and withholds it from the cell it did — feeding `n_positive_cells` and the `gap_fill` disjunct of `regime_met` at the PAPER→PROBE gate, the first time an autonomously-adopted strategy takes real capital. Two things make it nastier than its two closed siblings: it is the only one of the three NOT a partition key, so no mis-partitioning symptom ever surfaces to hint at it; and it can move the gate in BOTH directions, where `incubation_day` and `event_ts` fail only toward a false REJECT — a run-day cell that happens to be a fresh one raises `n_positive_cells` and can graduate a candidate on breadth it never demonstrated. Read alongside the `regime_met` vacuity pin in STEP 2 (2026-08-25): that one says the `>= 2` breadth bar cannot bind on the first graduation, and this one says the evidence feeding it can be wrong — same gate, independent defects.
     - **FALLBACK, deliberately the fail-safe one: if the processed day has no `SPY_TREND`/`VIX_REGIME` pair in `events.regime_events`, write `regime_cell = NULL`** (the column is nullable in BOTH tables STEP 1 writes it to — **`bigquery/35` carries a `regime_cell STRING` line TWICE, once inside its `CREATE TABLE … events.shadow_positions` block and once inside its `CREATE TABLE … analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` block, and neither is `NOT NULL`; disambiguated 2026-08-31 (SL3), which is why the anchor now names the enclosing block rather than the bare column line.** A bare grep for `regime_cell STRING` resolves to two different tables — the same two-places-not-one hazard the 2026-08-24 sweep sharpened `not_already_transitioned` for, and the only anchor in this block that still carried it; the underlying claim is unaffected, since the fallback is legitimate against both) and say so in the `<note>`. Do NOT substitute the run day's cell, and do NOT carry the nearest neighbouring day's forward. The coverage view already handles NULL explicitly (`WHERE p.phase = 'paper' AND p.regime_cell IS NOT NULL`), so a NULL drops the row out of the grid and UNDERSTATES breadth, whereas a substituted cell MANUFACTURES it — and manufactured breadth is precisely the failure `regime_met` exists to prevent. Understating is the SISA default-REJECT direction. The case is genuinely reachable: STEP 1's catch-up loop keys on days carrying a `state.daily_marks_curated` row, and D2a's regime write is a separate step from its marks ingest, so a day can in principle carry marks and no regime pair.
     - Closed BEFORE its first firing — `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` and `events.shadow_positions` both hold **0 rows** (verified live 2026-08-27), so no historical row needs remapping, the same position as the `incubation_day`, `event_ts`, `SHADOW->PAPER` and `->REJECTED` pins. **Prose only: no live SQL, no view and no gate is changed by this revision**, per the same posture as the 2026-08-25 `regime_met` referral — an incubation-monitor fire hardens its own read side and does not unilaterally edit a capital-adjacent gate.
   - **THE DAY-SET LOWER BOUND IS EXCLUSIVE ON THE LAST COMPLETION'S `run_date`, AND A FIRE WHOSE `run_date` EXCEEDS THE NEWEST DAY IT PROCESSED THEREFORE DROPS A TRADING DAY OUT OF THE SERIES PERMANENTLY — READ IT AS INCLUSIVE (SL3 2026-09-22 diligence sweep; the mechanism measured live against `state.daily_marks_curated` ingest times and `ops.run_log`, not inferred).** The loop above scopes its day set to trading days "between SL3's own last successful completion ... and today's `last_trading_day`" that carry a `state.daily_marks_curated` row, and every fire on record has read that lower bound as STRICTLY GREATER THAN the last completion's `run_date`. **That is safe if and only if a fire's `run_date` equals the newest day it processes, and until tonight it always did.** Measured: D2a ingests trading day X's marks at ~16:45 MT on X itself (09-08 16:45, 09-09 16:46, 09-10 16:46, 09-14 16:48, 09-15 16:47, 09-16 16:44, 09-17 16:46 — the only two exceptions in the window are catch-up writes, 09-11 landing 09-13 16:52 and 09-18 landing 09-20 16:47), and SL3's own evening slot runs ~20:10 MT on X, so an ordinary fire stamps `run_date` X having just processed X. A Sunday fire looks like a counter-example, and the ORDINARY Sunday EVENING fire genuinely is not one — **but the reason recorded here for dismissing it was FALSE, and it was the only thing standing between this pin and the one case where the hazard survives its own fix (corrected 2026-09-22 evening, SL3; the marks lag re-measured live against `events.daily_marks` ingest times, not inferred).** It read “the days it skips carry no marks”. They do: **every Friday's marks land on the FOLLOWING SUNDAY at ~16:47 MT** — 2026-08-28 ingested 2026-08-30 16:46, 09-04 → 09-06 16:52, 09-11 → 09-13 16:52, 09-18 → 09-20 16:47, four Fridays for four, D2a's own Sun-Thu cadence leaving each Friday to the next Sunday's fire (the same fact this block already states behind the DEPENDENCY GATE's UPSTREAM-HALT LIMB, where it is cited to show SL3 needs no place in a replay order — so the measurement was already in this block, one STEP away from the sentence it refutes). What actually makes an ordinary Sunday EVENING fire safe is nothing about Friday's marks and everything about WHERE THE BOUND SITS: its predecessor completed on THURSDAY, so the bound is Thursday and Friday sits comfortably inside the range by the time the ~20:10 MT slot reads it. **That reasoning does not survive a Sunday fire whose predecessor completed the SAME Sunday — see the PRE-OPEN SUNDAY limb below, which is the case this false premise concealed.**

The two were never reconciled because no fire had ever needed them to be.
     - **THE FIX THIS FIRE LANDED WAS PROSE AND THIS BLOCK'S OWN, AND IT IS STATED IN THE PAST TENSE FROM 2026-09-24 (SL3) BECAUSE ITS ANCHOR WAS SUPERSEDED TWO BULLETS BELOW WHILE THIS SENTENCE WENT ON GIVING IT AS THE LIVE INSTRUCTION: read the lower bound as INCLUSIVE — at the time, every trading day with `mark_date >= <SL3's last successful completion's `run_date`>` and `<= last_trading_day` that carries a `state.daily_marks_curated` row, oldest first. **THE INCLUSIVE READING SURVIVES AND IS STILL IN FORCE; THE `run_date` ANCHOR DOES NOT** — the live bound is the PER-MEMBER `COALESCE` two bullets below, so a session reading STEP 1 top-to-bottom must not execute this sentence. It is corrected here rather than left to the bullets below to override, on this block's own rule that a drifted pointer is worse than none: those bullets are marked as corrections OF this one, but this one was the only place the bound was ever stated as a plain imperative, and an imperative refuted 300 lines later is still the first thing a reader obeys.** The overlap that re-admits costs nothing and is already paid for: STEP 1's two idempotency guards key on `(strategy_code, phase, incubation_day)` and on `(strategy_code, event_type, ticker, DATE(event_ts))`, so a day already written is skipped row by row rather than re-inserted — precisely what those guards exist for, and the 2026-08-26 `event_ts` pin is what makes the second of them actually bind on a backfilled day rather than being vacuous. It also lands where the shared preamble was already pointing: the CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW rule says `state.routine_catchup_window.window_start_ts` "only ever widens the phrase, never narrows it", and that timestamp is the last completion's INSTANT — `2026-09-21T02:22:54Z` this run, i.e. 2026-09-20 20:22 MT, whose DATE is 09-20 and is therefore already inclusive of the last completed `run_date`. **So the exclusive reading was a NARROWING this block introduced on its own, against a shared rule that forbids narrowing**, which is why the fix is a correction rather than a new policy choice.
     - **DIRECTION AND BLAST RADIUS, because they are what make this a pin rather than a footnote.** The loss is SILENT and total for the affected day: no error, no gap flag, nothing to alert on. `shadow_days` and `paper_days` are `COUNT(DISTINCT incubation_day)`, so a dropped day simply never counts toward the 20-day SHADOW or 60-day PAPER floors; `excess_met`'s `ARRAY_AGG(excess ORDER BY incubation_day DESC LIMIT 10)` silently closes over a different ten days; and `state.arsenal_regime_coverage`'s `demonstrated` CTE never sees that day's cell. That is the same failure direction the `incubation_day` (2026-08-19) and `event_ts` (2026-08-26) pins already name as the one no other rail catches — a false REJECT of a sound candidate — reached by a THIRD route: not a day stamped with the wrong date, but a day never stamped at all. **Closed BEFORE its first firing**: `state.strategy_roster` carries 0 SHADOW and 0 PAPER members and `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf`, `events.shadow_positions` and `ops.roster_change_log` all hold 0 rows (all verified live this run), so the loop body was empty for 2026-09-21 and would have been empty for 2026-09-22 — no day's evidence was actually lost and no historical row needs remapping, the same position as the `SHADOW->PAPER` marker, the `->REJECTED` marker, the `regime_cell` pins and the `PENDING_ROSTER` lane. **Prose only — no SQL, no view, no threshold, no gate and no declaration is changed, and SL3 owns STEP 1, so no referral is owed and none is manufactured.**
     - **WHAT FOUND IT, because neither prescribed re-audit could have, and that is the reusable part.** The citation re-audit resolves pins against the tree, and every object named here exists and says what it is said to say. The block-wide executable-template census (prescribed 2026-09-15) compiles the templates this block gives — and **the day-set bound is a prose RANGE with no template**, exactly the blind spot the 2026-09-16 heartbeat finding named when it observed that "the instruction that executes most often is the one least likely to carry a template, precisely because nothing ever broke". The row census (2026-09-16) cannot see it either: the rows that would evidence it are the ones that were never written. What found it was deriving this fire's own day set and then asking what the NEXT fire would see given the `run_date` this fire is about to stamp. **Extend the re-resolve once more: each fire, re-measure the referrals, the quotations, the executable templates, the controlled-vocabulary columns — AND step this STEP's own watermark forward one fire, checking that every trading day between today and that next fire still falls inside some future day set.** A watermark defect is invisible to every instrument that inspects the present; only simulating the successor exposes it.
     - **AND SIMULATING THE SUCCESSOR IS EXACTLY WHAT FOUND THAT THE FIX ABOVE IS INCOMPLETE — THE INCLUSIVE BOUND CLOSES A ONE-DAY GAP AND THE PRE-OPEN SLOT CAN OPEN A TWO-DAY ONE, WHICH DROPS THE PRECEDING FRIDAY PERMANENTLY (SL3 2026-09-22 evening diligence sweep; the successor simulated on this fire's own `run_date`, the marks lag measured live).** The 2026-09-22 pre-open fire anchored the repaired bound on the last completion's **`run_date`**, which buys exactly ONE day of margin — enough for the gap that fire actually had (`run_date` 09-22, newest day processed 09-21), and that is why the fix reads as complete. **It is not, because the quantity the bound must never outrun is not the `run_date` but the newest day the previous fire actually PROCESSED, and the pre-open slot is precisely the class where those two come apart — by construction, since at ~04:17 MT today's session has not opened and today's marks do not exist.** Take the worked case, every limb of which is measured rather than supposed: SL3 completes Thursday evening (bound Thursday, newest processed Thursday); Friday carries no SL3 fire at all, SL3 being Sun-Thu; the Sunday EVENING slot is lost — a live class, not a hypothetical, since the Sunday 2026-09-20 W5 slot died to the account five-hour limit and the whole 2026-09-21 wave died to the OAuth expiry — and OPS2 hosts a PRE-OPEN catch-up that Sunday at ~04:17 MT. D2a has not yet run that Sunday (its slot is ~16:47 MT), so **Friday's marks are not curated yet** and the pre-open fire's day set is `{Thursday}`: newest processed Thursday, `run_date` Sunday, **a two-day gap**. Friday's marks land at ~16:47 MT that same Sunday. Every later fire then reads the repaired bound as `mark_date >= Sunday`, and **Friday — a trading day whose marks exist, sitting strictly below the bound — is never evaluated by any SL3 fire, ever.** The loss is the same silent, total, false-REJECT-direction one the bullets above describe, reached by a FOURTH route: not a day stamped with the wrong date, not a day never stamped at all because no fire covered it, but a day the watermark JUMPED OVER because the watermark measures the wrong thing. **THE FIX IS PROSE AND IT IS THIS BLOCK'S OWN: anchor the lower bound on the newest day SL3 actually PROCESSED, never on a `run_date`.** Concretely — and the GRAIN is the whole of it: the bound is **PER MEMBER**, computed for each SHADOW/PAPER member as `COALESCE(<`MAX(incubation_day)` in `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` **FOR THAT `strategy_code`**>, <that member's own SHADOW-entry `events.strategy_lifecycle` row date>)`, read inclusively, so a member's series can never begin after the day it entered SHADOW and never after the newest day SL3 actually stamped FOR IT. **NEVER a table-wide `MAX(incubation_day)`, and never a bare `LEAST(...)` over a watermark that can be NULL — both traps are measured in the grain bullet below, and the second one silently empties the day set.** **The bound then cannot outrun the evidence SL3 itself wrote FOR THAT MEMBER, which is what makes it self-healing in the one direction that matters:** any day SL3 failed to stamp for a member stays inside THAT member's every future day set until it IS stamped, whereas a `run_date` watermark advances whether or not the work was done. The scan stays bounded — a member's own watermark can only ever move its bound BACKWARD from today's, and the idempotency guards already make the re-admitted days free. **Closed BEFORE its first firing**, the same position as every other pin in this STEP: `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` holds 0 rows and `state.strategy_roster` carries 0 SHADOW and 0 PAPER members (both re-verified live this fire), so no series has a watermark to correct and no historical row needs remapping. **Prose only — no SQL, no view, no threshold, no gate and no declaration is changed, and SL3 owns STEP 1, so no referral is owed and none is manufactured.** **The method note is the reusable half, and it sharpens the instrument the previous fire prescribed rather than merely re-running it:** that fire added “step this STEP's own watermark forward one fire” and then simulated the successor of the fire IT was — an evening-slot successor, where the one-day margin holds. Simulating a successor is not one test but a FAMILY of them, one per slot class this routine can fire from, and the pre-open class had existed for less than a day when the instrument was written. **Extend it: simulate the successor from every slot class the routine can fire from — evening, pre-open catch-up, and OPS2-hosted inline — not only from the slot this fire happens to occupy.** A fix validated against the case that produced it is validated against a sample of one.
     - **AND THE REPAIRED BOUND WAS STATED AT THE WRONG GRAIN — A TABLE-WIDE `MAX` OVER A PER-MEMBER SERIES — SO THE SELF-HEALING PROPERTY IT CLAIMS HOLDS FOR THE HEALTHIEST MEMBER AND FAILS FOR EVERY LAGGING ONE; AND ITS DEGENERATE CASE EVALUATES TO NULL AND EMPTIES THE DAY SET OUTRIGHT (SL3 2026-09-23 diligence sweep; both limbs reproduced against BigQuery, not inferred).** The 2026-09-22 evening fix reads "the bound is `LEAST(<last completion's `run_date`>, <`MAX(incubation_day)` in `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf`>)` ... and for a member with no row in that table yet, its bound is the date of its own SHADOW-entry row". **The primary form is UNQUALIFIED — an aggregate over the WHOLE table — while the fallback beside it is PER MEMBER, and the two grains were never reconciled.** That is not a wording quibble: the bullet's own justification ("any day SL3 failed to stamp stays inside every future day set until it IS stamped") is TRUE per member and FALSE of a table-wide `MAX`, because the max is taken over ALL members, so **the furthest-along member's watermark sets the bound for everyone and a lagging member's unstamped days fall permanently BELOW it.** The fallback does not rescue that member — it is scoped to "a member with **no** row in that table yet", and this member has rows, just not recent ones. **It is reachable rather than exotic:** `k_incubate = 2` makes two concurrent incubating members the designed steady state rather than an edge case, the two normally enter incubation at different dates, and any per-member gap — a fire that died part-way through the member loop, which the 2026-09-21 OAuth expiry proves is a live class — is enough to open the divergence. **THE DEGENERATE LIMB IS THE CURRENT STATE, NOT A HYPOTHETICAL, AND IT IS QUIETER STILL.** GoogleSQL's `LEAST` is NULL-propagating: measured live this fire, `LEAST(DATE '2026-09-23', CAST(NULL AS DATE))` returns **NULL**, and a day-set predicate `mark_date >= <that NULL>` selects **0** rows — an EMPTY day set, with no error to notice. `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` holds **0 rows today**, so the table-wide `MAX(incubation_day)` **is NULL right now**, and it is NULL again on the first fire after the first SHADOW member is ever registered — the exact fire on which that member's series must begin. A session that computes the primary form as written gets an empty day set, writes no perf row, and leaves the watermark NULL for the next fire: **a self-perpetuating stall that no instrument in this block would see**, since the rows that would evidence it are the ones never written. **DIRECTION AND BLAST RADIUS:** the same silent, total, false-REJECT-direction loss the three preceding pins name — `shadow_days`/`paper_days` are `COUNT(DISTINCT incubation_day)`, `excess_met` closes over a different trailing ten days, and the coverage grid never sees the cell — reached by a FIFTH route: not a day stamped with the wrong date, not a day no fire covered, not a day the watermark jumped over, but **a day excluded by a DIFFERENT member's progress**. **THE FIX IS PROSE AND IT IS THIS BLOCK'S OWN**, applied at the site above: the bound is computed per member as `COALESCE(<`MAX(incubation_day)` for THAT `strategy_code`>, <that member's SHADOW-entry date>)` — a `COALESCE` rather than a `LEAST` precisely so a NULL watermark falls through to the entry date instead of annihilating the bound, and the `run_date` term is dropped as redundant, since `MAX(incubation_day)` can never exceed the `run_date` of the fire that wrote it. **WHAT FOUND IT, and it sharpens the instrument the previous fire prescribed rather than merely re-running it:** that fire extended the successor simulation to "every slot class the routine can fire from", and this fire ran it — but **every successor simulation this block has ever run, across all of them, was against a ZERO-member roster**, where the loop body is empty and the GRAIN of the bound is unobservable by construction. A bound's grain cannot be wrong until two members disagree about it. **Extend it once more: simulate the successor at every roster CARDINALITY the rails permit — 0, 1 and `k_incubate` = 2 members — not only the zero-member one the arsenal has been in for its entire history.** **Closed BEFORE its first firing**, the same position as every other pin in this STEP: `state.strategy_roster` carries 0 SHADOW and 0 PAPER members and `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf`, `events.shadow_positions` and `ops.roster_change_log` all hold 0 rows (all re-verified live this fire), so no member has a watermark to correct and no historical row needs remapping. **Prose only — no SQL, no view, no threshold, no gate and no declaration is changed, and SL3 owns STEP 1, so no referral is owed and none is manufactured.**
     - **AND THE REPAIR AT THE RIGHT GRAIN LEFT THE LOOP AT THE WRONG ONE — STEP 1'S NESTING WAS DAY-OUTER / MEMBER-INNER, WHICH PRESUPPOSES A SINGLE SHARED DAY SET THAT A PER-MEMBER BOUND NO LONGER PROVIDES; AND THE TWO WAYS TO MANUFACTURE ONE FAIL IN OPPOSITE DIRECTIONS, THE LIKELIER OF THEM TOWARD A FALSE GRADUATION (SL3 2026-09-24 diligence sweep; found by the cardinality-2 successor simulation the bullet above prescribed, on its first outing).** The 2026-09-23 fix moved the BOUND to per-member grain and stopped there. STEP 1's opening sentence still read "for EVERY missed trading day between ... — oldest first — run the SHADOW/PAPER bullets below against THAT day's marks ... For every SHADOW/PAPER strategy in `state.strategy_roster`" — days in the outer loop, members in the inner one. **At cardinality 0 or 1 the two grains are indistinguishable and the loop reads correctly; at cardinality 2 with divergent watermarks there IS no single day set to put in the outer loop, and `k_incubate = 2` is the designed steady state rather than an edge case.** A session executing the nesting as written must therefore reduce the per-member bounds to one date, and **both reductions are wrong, in OPPOSITE directions:** `MAX` over members re-opens verbatim the defect the bullet above closed — the furthest-along member's watermark excludes a lagging member's unstamped days, permanently and silently. **`MIN` over members is the reading a session would actually reach for, having just been warned off `MAX` one bullet earlier, and it is the worse of the two.** It evaluates every member from the EARLIEST member's bound, so a later-entering member gets `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` rows stamped for days BEFORE it entered SHADOW. **That is not an accounting inflation, it is a category error, and naming it as one is the point of this bullet.** SISA freezes a strategy's OWN machinery at SHADOW entry (§STRATEGY ARSENAL LIFECYCLE preamble; the edge clock starts at its first PROBE trade), so a perf row for a pre-entry day is measured against a spec that was not frozen on that day — **it is a BACKTEST row filed in the forward-test series**, which is the one thing SHADOW exists to prevent. It then counts: `shadow_days` and `paper_days` are `COUNT(DISTINCT incubation_day)`, so those rows push a member toward the 20-day and 60-day floors on days it was not incubating, and `excess_met`'s `ARRAY_AGG(excess ORDER BY incubation_day DESC LIMIT 10)` closes its trailing window over them. **This is the FIRST failure this STEP has recorded that runs toward a false GRADUATION** — the `incubation_day` (08-19), `event_ts` (08-26), `regime_cell` (08-27), watermark (09-22) and grain (09-23) pins all fail toward a false REJECT, which the SISA default-REJECT bias absorbs; this one is not absorbed by anything, and it lands at the gate that spends money. **THE FIX IS PROSE AND IT IS THIS BLOCK'S OWN: invert the nesting — MEMBERS outer, that member's OWN day set inner — applied at the STEP 1 sentence above, and never reduce the per-member bounds to a single date.** **AND IT PROMOTES THE `COALESCE` FALLBACK FROM A NULL-GUARD TO A RAIL, which the bullet above does not say and which is the reusable half.** That bullet justifies `COALESCE` over `LEAST` solely so a NULL watermark falls through instead of annihilating the bound. The SHADOW-entry date is also **the floor that keeps the forward test FORWARD** — a member's series can never begin before the day it entered SHADOW — and that is precisely the property a shared day set destroys for every member except the earliest-entering one. Read the fallback as both things; a future widening that keeps the NULL-guard and loses the floor loses the rail. **ONE RESIDUAL, MEASURED AND DELIBERATELY NOT FILED.** The per-member bound's remaining exposure is marks for day Q arriving AFTER SL3 has already stamped some day P > Q for that member: the watermark then sits above Q and Q is lost. It requires OUT-OF-ORDER marks ingest, and that has never happened — measured live this fire across the whole of `state.daily_marks_curated`, **111 mark days back to 2026-04-17 and ZERO pairs** in which a `mark_date` first ingested later than its predecessor did. The sibling case is closed by construction rather than by luck: oldest-first processing means a fire that dies part-way through a member's day set has stamped a PREFIX of it, so the bound lands on the last day it completed and every later day is re-admitted — which is why the 2026-09-21 OAuth-expiry class, a proven live failure mode, does not reach this. Recorded as a bounded residual, not a defect: nothing is fixable while ingest is monotone, and a rail against a 0-for-111 event would cost more than it buys. **Closed BEFORE its first firing**, the same position as every other pin in this STEP: `state.strategy_roster` carries 0 SHADOW and 0 PAPER members and `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf`, `events.shadow_positions` and `ops.roster_change_log` all hold 0 rows (all re-verified live this fire), so no member's series has ever been computed from a shared day set and no historical row needs remapping. **Prose only — no SQL, no view, no threshold, no gate and no declaration is changed, and SL3 owns STEP 1, so no referral is owed and none is manufactured.** **WHAT FOUND IT, and it sharpens the instrument rather than merely re-running it.** The 2026-09-23 fire prescribed simulating the successor at every roster CARDINALITY the rails permit, on the reasoning that "a bound's grain cannot be wrong until two members disagree about it". That is true and it is one level too narrow: at the cardinality where the BOUND's grain becomes observable, so does the grain of **the loop that consumes it**, and the loop is where a grain mismatch turns into a wrong write rather than a wrong date. **Extend it once more: at each cardinality, simulate the LOOP and not only the bound — ask what quantity a session must compute to execute the nesting as written, and whether that quantity exists at that cardinality at all.** A bound stated correctly and consumed at the wrong grain is indistinguishable, from inside the bound, from a correct one.
     - **AND THE BOUND'S FALLBACK TERM NAMES A ROW IN THE SINGULAR WHERE THE SCHEMA PERMITS MANY, AND SAYS NOTHING ABOUT WHICH ONE GOVERNS (SL3 2026-09-27 diligence sweep; the DDL and the constraint set measured, not inferred).** The per-member bound above, and the three bullets that built it, each write "<that member's own SHADOW-entry `events.strategy_lifecycle` row date>" — four occurrences, every one definite, none qualified. **`events.strategy_lifecycle` is append-only with `PRIMARY KEY (event_id) NOT ENFORCED` over a `GENERATE_UUID()` key and no constraint of any kind on `(strategy_code, to_state)`** (`bigquery/35_strategy_arsenal.sql`'s `CREATE TABLE … events.strategy_lifecycle` block), and `state.strategy_roster` takes only the LATEST row per code — so a second `to_state='SHADOW'` row for one `strategy_code` is accepted silently, and nothing in this STEP says whether the bound then takes the first or the last. **Take the LATEST `to_state='SHADOW'` row for that member, by `(event_ts DESC, event_id DESC)`** — the same tiebreaker the `regime_cell` pin above uses, and for the same reason. **`MIN` is the wrong answer, and it is wrong in the now-familiar direction:** it would start a re-entering member's series at the day it FIRST entered SHADOW, admitting the whole of a prior, already-rejected episode plus the gap between them as forward-test days — the identical BACKTEST-ROW-IN-THE-FORWARD-TEST category error the nesting bullet above names for `MIN` over MEMBERS, reached here over EPISODES of one member. It is one clause, so it is corrected here rather than referred. **The half that is NOT SL3's rides the same `ops.alerts` `info` row STEP 4 raises this fire** (`incubation_cooldown_rail_unenforced_in_sql`): `state.strategy_shadow_readiness`, `state.strategy_paper_readiness` and `state.arsenal_regime_coverage` each aggregate `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` with a bare `GROUP BY strategy_code` and no episode boundary (`bigquery/60`'s `agg` CTE, `bigquery/189`'s, and `bigquery/35`'s `demonstrated` CTE), and no cull deletes or supersedes a perf row — measured repo-wide this fire, there is no `DELETE FROM` or `UPDATE` against `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` or `events.shadow_positions` anywhere — so a reused `strategy_code` would blend episodes at every gate at once. **Reachability is honest and bounded, and is stated so a later sweep does not overrate it: the designed re-entry path mints a NEW candidate code** (`state.strategy_candidates.is_restart_of`, "terminated strategy_code this restarts"), which makes the blend unreachable by design — but `candidate_code` carries **no enforced uniqueness**, a fact `bigquery/60`'s own `freq` CTE comment states outright ("candidate_code has no enforced uniqueness and four independent routines write NEW rows"), so it is prevented by convention only. **Closed BEFORE its first firing** — 0 SHADOW and 0 PAPER members, 0 perf rows, and no `strategy_code` has ever held two episodes (all re-verified live this fire). **Prose only — no SQL, no view, no threshold, no gate and no declaration is changed.**

## H-SL3-03 — STEP 2 — EVALUATE READINESS (objective views; the PAPER view encodes the antichurn caps — the SHADOW view deli

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (SL3, was line 5308) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

STEP 2 — EVALUATE READINESS (objective views; the PAPER view encodes the anti-churn caps — the SHADOW view deliberately encodes none, see the term-naming pin below — and each encodes a NOT-EXISTS-later-state idempotency guard against `ops.roster_change_log`).
   - `state.strategy_shadow_readiness.ready` = `days_met` AND signal rate within the declared band AND zero scaffolding faults AND `arsenal_ok` AND not-already-transitioned.
     - **THE FIRST CONJUNCT USED TO READ `>= min_shadow_trading_days`, WHICH NAMES A POLICY RECORD NOTHING READS RATHER THAN THE VIEW COLUMN IT DESCRIBES (SL3 2026-09-06 diligence sweep; measured against the live view body and `scripts/`, not inferred).** This is the residue of the same defect the 2026-09-03 sweep corrected two slots away, in a milder form: `caps_ok` and `theater_ok` were names of a DIFFERENT view, while `min_shadow_trading_days` is a name from a different LAYER. It is not a phantom — it is real at `strategy/roster.yaml`'s `rails:` block, where it reads `min_shadow_trading_days: 20` — but **nothing resolves it to anything.** The number that actually governs SHADOW graduation is a bare literal `20` compiled into the view (`COALESCE(a.shadow_days, 0) >= 20 AS days_met`, live in `bigquery/60_shadow_stuck_cull.sql` and byte-identical in `dbt/models/state/strategy_shadow_readiness.sql`), which reads no table, no config and no roster value. The `>= 2` breadth bar therefore cannot bind on the one decision it was written for — the FIRST PAPER→PROBE graduation, the first time an autonomously-adopted strategy takes real capital. Worse, `excess_met` already demands cumulative paper excess `>= 0` on each of the trailing 10 incubation days, so a candidate that clears `excess_met` has almost certainly already cleared the reduced `regime_met`: as it stands the term adds close to nothing to the gate. **Why all nine cells are gaps, and why it is not a defect in the view:** `is_gap` is `covered_active_count = 0` (the `cov` CTE's `COUNTIF(r.is_active AND d.positive_excess)` over the `demonstrated` CTE, which reads `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` `phase='paper'` rows ONLY), and SL3 STEP 1 is that table's ONLY writer anywhere in the repo, writing solely for members currently in SHADOW/PAPER. The five ADOPTED members A–E were seeded straight to `ADOPTED` at experiment start by `bigquery/35`'s founding-batch seed block and carry no SHADOW/PAPER history, so they can never enter `demonstrated`; `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` holds **0 rows** (verified live 2026-08-25, as is the byte-identity of the live `state.arsenal_regime_coverage` body with `bigquery/35`'s). `bigquery/35`'s own header already says so outright — "the ADOPTED-coverage measurement leg … needs a technical-regime daily series that does not yet exist — and is deliberately NOT built here" — and names the SL1-synthesis bias as the consequence. **What was never written down is the consequence AT THIS GATE, which is the one that spends money.** Note the shape: the vacuity is self-limiting (once a graduate is `is_active`, the cells it demonstrated stop being gaps for the NEXT candidate) and therefore bites hardest on the first graduation and decays thereafter — the exact inverse of the profile you would want. **A future SL3 must NOT read `regime_met = TRUE` as evidence of demonstrated regime BREADTH; read `n_positive_cells` directly and say which it is in the `<note>`.** And do **NOT** repair this from an SL3 fire: changing `is_gap`, `regime_met`, or the coverage view's inputs is a live-SQL change to a capital-adjacent gate whose blast radius also covers SL1's zero-coverage synthesis bias, and an incubation-monitor run is the wrong place to decide it unilaterally — the same posture, and for the same reason, as the 2026-08-17 AR_orc `EMBEDDING-LAG` finding that hardened its read side in prose and left the gate SQL alone. Different object, different drainer, different decision. So a count that aged did not simply go stale here — **it omitted the single lane most capable of misrouting the very referral the sentence is about**, and it would have done so silently, since a reader who finds four lanes listed and none fitting concludes correctly, while a reader who finds the fifth for himself has no text telling him it is not the one. **Do NOT route a `regime_met` (or any graduation-gate) finding to `PENDING_REGIME_REFRESH`.** Prose only; no lane, view, gate or threshold is changed, and no referral is owed — this is SL3's own text.

That check is real and it is load-bearing, but it binds one stage EARLIER, at adoption into SHADOW, where the candidate carries no capital at all; by `PAPER->PROBE` it is long spent and nothing re-tests it. STEP 2's own preamble sentence claimed "each encodes the anti-churn caps", which is TRUE of the PAPER view (`rate_limit_clear`, `ceiling_ok`) and FALSE of the SHADOW view; that preamble is the most likely generator of the wrong term, and it is corrected in the same revision.
