The label is the one part of a cross-file quotation this block had never re-resolved — the 2026-09-10 and 2026-09-22 sweeps both re-resolved the quoted TEXT, which is verbatim-accurate and always has been, and neither checked WHERE it was. A wrong section label is the prose twin of the drifted `:NNN` this block rules against: it points a session at real prose that is not the prose meant, and the misdirection survives every audit that greps for the quoted words.**) — "`state.arsenal_rails` has no `caps_ok` column; that name exists only on `state.strategy_adoption_readiness`, a later-stage view" — so this exact name, mis-homed onto a different object, was found and pinned in a neighbouring section while the instance here survived. **A correction landed in one routine's section does not sweep its siblings, and no CI check could have swept this one:** `scripts/check_handoff_contracts.py`'s `required_column_naming` check verifies that every `NOT NULL`-with-no-default column of a declared WRITE target is named in this document, and a readiness view is READ-ONLY to SL3 — the whole mechanical guard is on the write side, so a mis-named read term is invisible to it by construction. **Closed BEFORE its first firing**: both views hold 0 rows and no strategy has ever entered SHADOW or PAPER, so no graduation was ever decided on the wrong reading. **Prose only — no view, no gate, no threshold and no SQL is changed by this revision**; the SQL was right and the text describing it was wrong, which is the inverse of the usual drift and the reason it survived four diligence sweeps that all re-audited the WRITE contracts.

## H-SL3-04 — STEP 3 — SELFEXECUTE / HAND OFF.

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (SL3, was line 5321) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

STEP 3 — SELF-EXECUTE / HAND OFF.
   - SHADOW→PAPER ready: write the PAPER `events.strategy_lifecycle` row YOURSELF (light transition — no repo/roster/capital change) — `strategy_code` (`NOT NULL`, no default), `from_state='SHADOW'`, `to_state='PAPER'`, `driver_routine='SL3'`, one-line `note`; `event_id`/`event_ts` default. **THEN, AND LAST, WRITE THE `ops.roster_change_log` MARKER — `change_key='<code>:SHADOW->PAPER'` (that EXACT literal), `strategy_code='<code>'`, `from_state='SHADOW'`, `to_state='PAPER'`, one-line `note`. PINNED 2026-08-19 (SL3 diligence sweep, handoff-wiring class) — and this one is not a restatement of an existing convention, it closes a marker with NO WRITER ANYWHERE IN THE SYSTEM.** That literal is the `not_already_transitioned` term — and a conjunct of `ready` itself — in all ~~three~~ **FOUR** generations of `state.strategy_shadow_readiness` (`bigquery/35`'s `not_already_transitioned` alias and the same term inside its `ready`, `bigquery/52`:79/87, `bigquery/60_shadow_stuck_cull.sql`:69/91 (live until 2026-10-25), the CURRENT live definition `bigquery/266_shadow_paper_restored.sql`'s `not_already_transitioned` alias and the same term inside its `ready` (grep together with the `':SHADOW->PAPER'` literal — `bigquery/266` also carries the `UNDER_REVIEW->SHADOW` one; a fifth generation and these repins all added 2026-10-25 by SL3's citation re-audit) — **and `dbt/models/state/strategy_shadow_readiness.sql`:46/68, the fourth, corrected 2026-08-24 (SL3)**). **That fourth one is not a historical generation like the first two — it is a live, CI-guarded parallel definition** carrying the identical `CONCAT(r.strategy_code, ':SHADOW->PAPER')` term in BOTH `not_already_transitioned` and `ready`, plus its own copy of the `stuck` flag mirroring `bigquery/60`'s. It matters here for one reason: the `dbt-parity` CI job (CLAUDE.md's named guard for SQL-layer drift) compares the dbt models against the `bigquery/` definitions, so a session that reads "all three generations", edits the change_key term in the three it was told about, and pushes, turns `main` red on a job it was never pointed at — and `auto-merge` is fail-closed on CI with a single retry per tip SHA, which is the stranded-branch failure the Branch-and-state-propagation section documents as unrecoverable after the session ends. **Whenever this literal changes, change it in FOUR places.** A repo-wide grep across `bigquery/`, `scripts/`, `ops/`, `task_plan/`, **`dbt/`** and this file finds `SHADOW->PAPER` **only as a read, never once as a write** (**`dbt/` added to the stated scope 2026-08-24 (SL3) — see the fourth-generation correction just above; the original enumeration omitted it, and while re-running the grep with `dbt/` included changes the CONCLUSION not at all — that hit is a read too — a scope list that omits a directory containing live definitions of the very view under discussion cannot be relied on by the next session that re-runs it**): SL5's three pinned keys are `'<code>:UNDER_REVIEW->SHADOW'` (branch 1, read by `state.strategy_adoption_readiness`), `'<code>:PAPER->PROBE'` (branch 2, read by `state.strategy_paper_readiness`) and `'<code>:<FROM>->TERMINATED'` (branch 3) — **none of them is this one**, and SL4/AR_orc write only their own `ADOPTED<->RETIREMENT_PROPOSED` keys. SHADOW→PAPER is the single lifecycle transition SL3 self-executes, so SL3 is the only routine that could ever arm it, and it was never told to. **Correcting the record — and stated in the PAST tense from 2026-09-10 (SL3 diligence sweep), because the sentence it describes was repaired the same day it was diagnosed while this clause went on asserting otherwise for three weeks.** SL5's `change_key` pin (2026-08-19, its IDEMPOTENCY GUARD paragraph) ONCE listed `state.strategy_shadow_readiness` among the views "hardcoded" against SL5's branch-(1)/branch-(2) strings; that attribution was off by one link in the chain, and the mis-mapping is precisely what let this gap survive a sweep whose whole subject was these keys. **It no longer lists it, and SL5's own text says so in as many words** — that paragraph today carries a `CORRECTED 2026-08-19 (SL3 diligence sweep)` marker recording that "this sentence originally said THREE views and named `state.strategy_shadow_readiness` … among them", that the view's key "is NOT an SL5 branch at all", and that "SL5 must NOT write a `SHADOW->PAPER` key". **The repair was made by THIS block's own 2026-08-19 sweep: SL3 corrected the neighbouring section and then wrote a present-tense description of the uncorrected state into its own.** SL5's corrected text names the generating failure in the abstract one clause earlier — "the sweep that fixed the neighbouring clause did not re-run its own test one clause over" — and this sentence is that identical failure run in the opposite direction, across a section boundary, by the same sweep in the same hour. **Two things survive it.** The conclusion is untouched and still load-bearing: `SHADOW->PAPER` is the one transition SL3 self-executes, so SL3 is the only routine that could ever arm this marker, which is the whole point of the pin above. And the reusable half: **a claim about ANOTHER routine's prose is a live measurement with a short half-life, not a fixed citation.** STEP 5's per-fire re-resolve already re-measures the live claims a REFERRAL rests on; this is the same obligation on a cross-section QUOTATION, and nothing was re-measuring those. Re-read the quoted text before repeating a claim about it.
     - **AND THE LINE NUMBER INSIDE THAT CLAUSE WAS NEVER RESOLVABLE AT ALL — it pinned `state.strategy_shadow_readiness`:516.** SL5's paragraph carries no `:NNN` pin on that view in any form (it names it "(`bigquery/35`; live at `bigquery/60`)"), and 516 resolves to unrelated shared-preamble text in `Claude_Task_Plan.md`, `task_plan/SL5.md` and `task_plan/SL3.md`, which is every file the citation could plausibly have meant. **THAT SENTENCE ORIGINALLY NAMED WHICH TEXT, AND NAMED THE WRONG ONE IN ALL THREE FILES — corrected 2026-09-13 (SL3 diligence sweep; all three resolved line-by-line, not inferred).** It said 516 lands on "the `GO`/`NO-GO`/`DEFER` decision-vocabulary rule … alike". It does not, and the three files do not agree, so "alike" was wrong twice over: in the monolith that offset landed on the **`1. entry_type and decision are CONTROLLED VOCABULARIES. Do not coin a variant.`** bullet — the ADJACENT rule, with the `GO`/`NO-GO`/`DEFER` vocabulary line nine lines further down — while in BOTH slices it landed on a **blank line**. **THE 2026-09-13 CORRECTION RECORDED THOSE FINDINGS AS FOUR FRESH PROSE LINE NUMBERS (516 / 525 / 516 / 528) AND EVERY ONE OF THEM HAD DRIFTED WITHIN ONE DAY — so they are REMOVED here rather than renumbered (SL3 2026-09-14 diligence sweep; all three files re-resolved, not inferred).** Measured this run, the `CONTROLLED VOCABULARIES` bullet sits at monolith line **561** and at **564** in both slices, the vocabulary line nine lines below each; monolith line 516 now holds unrelated chat-output text, and only the "slice line 516 is blank" observation still coincidentally holds. The mover was commit `4c8d06d` on 2026-09-14, which added ~45 lines to the shared preamble (and ~48 to each slice) — i.e. **any edit anywhere ABOVE the cited text moves it, and the shared preamble is edited by the whole fleet.** **This is the single sharpest instance this block has produced of its own rule, because it is self-demonstrating: the clause whose entire subject is that a prose line-pin is unresolvable recorded four new prose line-pins in the act of withdrawing one, and they survived a day.** The rule stated two sentences below was therefore already violated by the sentence stating it. Corrected by naming the markers — the `CONTROLLED VOCABULARIES` bullet and the `GO`/`NO-GO`/`DEFER` vocabulary line, both inside §Decision-log lifecycle's write-discipline list — which is what makes the claim checkable without being datable. **The substantive conclusion is untouched and in fact strengthened: `:516` was never a resolvable pin, and it is less resolvable than this clause claimed, since in the two files a routine actually executes from it points at whitespace.** It is corrected because of what it is an instance of: **this is the sentence that WITHDRAWS an unresolvable citation, and it misdescribed its own evidence while doing so** — the same one-clause-over failure the STEP 3 text above names in the abstract ("the sweep that fixed the neighbouring clause did not re-run its own test one clause over"), committed inside the repair itself. A withdrawal is a measurement too: resolve the line you are declaring unresolvable, in every file you name, before saying what it lands on. It was also malformed in kind: `state.strategy_shadow_readiness` is a VIEW NAME, not a path, so the `:NNN` had no file to attach to even in principle. **It is the only `:NNN` citation this block has ever aimed at a PROSE document rather than a `bigquery/`, `dbt/` or `ops/` file, and that is exactly why the 2026-08-23 citation audit did not catch it:** that pass resolved its pins "against the tree" and its remedy was converting `bigquery/35`'s six drifted pins to named anchors — a code-shaped fix for a code-shaped problem, applied by an audit with no reason to look at the one pin pointing somewhere else. **Do not line-pin a prose document. Name the section and the marker, as this clause now does.** **Why it is worth a write rather than shrugging off as belt-and-braces:** the SL5 pin's own reasoning ("each of those views ALSO filters `WHERE r.current_state = '<prior state>'`, so the `change_key` term is belt-and-braces there") is TRUE here too — the PAPER lifecycle row drops the code out of `WHERE r.current_state = 'SHADOW'` (`bigquery/266`'s closing population filter; `bigquery/60`:96 in its superseded generation, repinned 2026-10-25 by SL3) immediately, which is why nothing has ever visibly broken. But that leaves the population filter as a SINGLE point of failure at exactly the seam where this document deliberately permits two SL3 sessions to overlap: the QUIESCENCE REBUTTAL lets a successor PROCEED past a predecessor's open `'started'` row on 45 minutes of BigQuery silence, and 45 minutes of silence is evidence of death, not proof of it. Two live sessions each reading `ready=TRUE` each append a PAPER row to an append-only log that is the audit record of a spec freeze — `state.strategy_roster` takes the latest row per code and looks fine, so the duplicate is invisible there and surfaces only as a double-counted transition in W5's arsenal digest. The marker is what makes the second writer a no-op instead. `ops.roster_change_log` holds **0 rows** as of this pin, so — like the `PENDING_ROSTER` lane (2026-08-18) and SL5's `change_key` strings (2026-08-19) — this is closed before its first firing and no historical row needs remapping.
   - PAPER→PROBE ready: enqueue an `events.queue_events` item for SL5 — **`queue='PENDING_ROSTER'`, `item_key='probe-register-<code>'`, `item_type='probe-register'`, `status='pending'`, `strategy='<code>'`, `due_date` = `state.trading_day_today.today`** — SL5 is the sole roster-membership writer and the only routine that runs the fanout / touches the funding queue. **Every one of those column values is PINNED here deliberately (SL5 2026-08-18 diligence sweep, handoff-wiring class).** **The item's TERMINAL status is SL5's, not SL3's: SL5 writes the `'complete'` `events.queue_events` row on drain (SL5 branch (2), pinned 2026-08-19) — the same drain-side close this lane's sibling `PENDING_DRAFT` contract states, added here so the lane is specified end-to-end (enqueue AND close) on both sides rather than only at its head.** Until then this line named only `item_type` plus a `trigger_context=strategy_code` that is not a column of `events.queue_events` at all (its columns are `event_id` and `event_ts` — both carrying defaults, which is why an enqueueing caller never supplies them — plus `queue, item_key, item_type, status, strategy, ticker, due_date, conservative_default, artifact_path, payload, note`; thirteen in all, `bigquery/01_schema.sql`:70-81. **The two defaulted columns were missing from this enumeration until 2026-08-24 (SL3): the list was written as the caller's-eye view but stated as "its columns are", so as a literal claim about the table it was wrong, in a line whose whole purpose is to be the authority on what is and is not a column of `events.queue_events`**), while `queue`, `item_key` **and `status`** are `NOT NULL` with no default (**`status` added 2026-09-22 evening (SL3), by the block-wide executable-template census rather than by a citation audit: the live table has THREE such columns, not the two this line named. No live defect follows — the template one clause above already supplies `status='pending'` — but STEP 5's own required-column re-verification already listed all three, so the block contradicted itself about a measured schema fact, in the direction that UNDERSTATES what a caller must supply. Same shape as the 33-vs-32 routine-count self-contradiction this block corrected on 2026-09-14, and the same remedy: re-derive from `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.COLUMNS`, never from the neighbouring prose**) and SL5's own scan named no lane either — so the first real PAPER→PROBE graduation would have had two independent sessions each invent a lane string, with a coin-flip chance SL5 never finds the row SL3 wrote and the graduation silently stalls with nothing to alert on. This is the same shape as the AR_orc→SL2 offset gap that cost two SL2 fires on 2026-08-16 (`ar_orc_revision_unrouted`, fixed 2026-08-17 commit d66da71), caught here BEFORE its first firing rather than after. `PENDING_ROSTER` follows the `PENDING_*` lane convention and is registered with drainer `['SL5']` in `state.queue_venue_claim_unwired`'s canonical queue→drainer map — a lane absent from that map is treated as "no legitimate venue claim is possible here", so registering it is what keeps a correct `resolving_venue='SL5'` payload from being flagged as unwired. (Verified live 2026-09-10: the map's five `STRUCT('<QUEUE>' AS queue, [...] AS allowed_drainers)` rows were read off the LIVE view body via `state.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.VIEWS`, and `PENDING_ROSTER`→`['SL5']` is among them.) **DO NOT PIN THE MAP'S DEFINING FILENAME HERE — corrected 2026-09-10 (SL3 diligence sweep), and the correction is the resolution RULE, deliberately not a newer number.** This clause named `bigquery/180_probe_register_queue_lane.sql` "superseding `bigquery/160`" as the map's home. That chain is two links short: `bigquery/199_queue_venue_claim_status_normalisation.sql` superseded 180, and `bigquery/225_regime_refresh_queue_lane.sql` superseded 199 on 2026-09-05 — four definitions of one view, three of them inside three weeks, so ANY number written here rots on the next lane added. `bigquery/*.sql` is apply-in-order and supersede-only and the `allowed_map` is a literal inside the view body rather than a table anything can INSERT into (`bigquery/225`'s own header states both), so the map's home is *by construction* **the highest-numbered `bigquery/*.sql` that CREATEs the view** — which is exactly how `scripts/check_handoff_contracts.py`'s `resolve_allowed_map_source()` finds it, pinning no filename. **This repository has already had this argument once, on this very object, and settled it the other way:** `ops/handoff_contracts.yaml`'s header records that its own comment "named bigquery/180 for a week after bigquery/199 superseded it, which is the drift the runtime resolution exists to prevent", and its response was to DELETE the pin, not to bump it. Resolve the file when you need it; do not name it. **The hazard here is not the usual one and it is not cosmetic.** A drifted `:NNN` points at the wrong line of the right file; this pointed at the wrong FILE — a landed, still-present, still-readable definition that is simply no longer the one in force — so a session sent here to inspect or extend the lane map would have edited a superseded body: a silent no-op against live, invisible to review, and a `scripts/check_live_sql_parity.py` divergence on an object that compares final-effective bodies only. **Swept this run, and the result is worth recording because a sweep that finds nothing elsewhere is also a result: SL3 is the ONLY site carrying this defect.** The three other `bigquery/180` citations in this document (AR_att once, SL5 twice) each cite that file for what its OWN header records — the unpinned-cross-routine-handoff defect class, the `handoff_contract_unpinned` `ops.alert_policy` registration, and its description of SL5's three dispatch inputs — claims about an immutable file's own contents, which a supersede does not touch. No referral is owed and none is manufactured. The eventual PROBE launch (in SL5) additionally respects `ops.trading_control` (an account-wide halt defers the LAUNCH, not incubation).

## H-SL3-05 — STEP 4 — CULL. On a windowexceeded / catastrophic break (SHADOW) or materiallynegative paper excess / paper dr

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (SL3, was line 5326) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

STEP 4 — CULL. On a window-exceeded / catastrophic break (SHADOW) or materially-negative paper excess / paper drawdown-kill-equivalent (PAPER), write a REJECTED `events.strategy_lifecycle` row + a `cooldown_until` stamp on the candidate. **THE STAMP'S ADDRESS IS PINNED (SL3 2026-08-19 diligence sweep): `UPDATE state.strategy_candidates SET cooldown_until = <today + `state.arsenal_rails.reject_cooldown_days` (=90)> WHERE candidate_code = '<code>'`** — `cooldown_until` is a `DATE` column of `state.strategy_candidates` (`bigquery/35`'s `cooldown_until DATE` column) and of no other table, and SL1 already stamps it the same way (`bigquery/133`'s `UPDATE state.strategy_candidates … WHERE candidate_code = 'G'` shows **G** carrying `2026-10-25`). **CORRECTED 2026-08-23 (SL3 citation audit): that parenthetical read "shows F/G carrying `2026-10-25`". It is right for G and wrong for F** — the cited statement is scoped `WHERE candidate_code = 'G'` alone, and `bigquery/133`'s own comment says F is deliberately untouched. F's `cooldown_until` value appears nowhere in the repo, so do not cite this file for it — read it live. **FURTHER CORRECTED 2026-08-24 (SL3): the 08-23 correction was right on the half that is about the FILE and wrong on the half that is about the WORLD, and the wrong half is the more dangerous of the two because it invites a session to "repair" data that is correct.** Its supporting inference — "F was rejected on 2026-07-28 and G on a later date, and the rail is a flat 90-day stamp, so the two dates cannot be equal in any case" — is FALSE on both limbs. F and G were rejected by the SAME SL1 run in the same statement batch: `events.strategy_lifecycle` carries both `CANDIDATE`→`REJECTED` rows at the IDENTICAL `event_ts` (`2026-07-28 00:10:29.279889` MT, `driver_routine='SL1'`), written by SL1's `run_date` 2026-07-27 fire — G is not "a later date", it is the same instant. A flat 90-day stamp off one `run_date` therefore MUST give the two the same value, and live `state.strategy_candidates` shows exactly that: **F and G both carry `2026-10-25`** (= 2026-07-27 + 90), with H at `2026-11-01` (= its own `run_date` 2026-08-03 + 90) independently corroborating the rail — all three verified live 2026-08-24. What survives from 08-23 is only the citation-scope point, which is sound and worth keeping: the `UPDATE` is scoped `WHERE candidate_code = 'G'` and `bigquery/133`'s own comment says F is deliberately untouched, so that file evidences nothing about F's value even though the value happens to match. **Equal `cooldown_until` dates across candidates culled in the same run are the rail working as specified, not a corruption — do NOT "correct" them, and do not read this line as licence to.** This is the citation-audit failure mode inverted: 08-23 audited what the FILE said and then reasoned about the WORLD without querying it, which is how a correction becomes the defect. "The candidate" was the only address this line gave; every OTHER `cooldown_until` consumer keys off that exact table, so a session that invented a different home for the stamp would leave the post-rejection cooldown unenforced and let a just-culled archetype re-enter incubation immediately. Set `reject_reason` and `status='REJECTED'` on the same row while you are there.
   - **AND THE REASON THAT PIN GIVES FOR ITSELF IS FALSE, IN THE DIRECTION THAT MAKES THE RAIL LOOK MECHANICAL WHEN IT IS PROSE: `cooldown_until` HAS NO READER IN SQL ANYWHERE, AND ITS ONLY CONSUMER IN THE SYSTEM IS ONE NARRATIVE BULLET IN SL1's STEP 3 THAT READS AT A DIFFERENT GRAIN THAN THIS STAMP WRITES (SL3 2026-09-27 diligence sweep; measured against the LIVE view and routine bodies via `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.VIEWS`/`ROUTINES` and repo-wide, not inferred).** The pin above closes: "every OTHER `cooldown_until` consumer keys off that exact table, so a session that invented a different home for the stamp would leave the post-rejection cooldown unenforced and let a just-culled archetype re-enter incubation immediately." **There is no other consumer; there is no mechanical enforcement at the pinned address either; and the one consumer that does exist keys off the ARCHETYPE rather than off the `candidate_code` this stamp is scoped to.**
     - **LIMB ONE — ZERO SQL READERS, MEASURED LIVE RATHER THAN BY GREP.** Every view body in `state`, `analytics`, `ops`, `events` and `perf`, and every `ops` routine body, was searched for the string `cooldown_until` this fire (`STRPOS(view_definition, 'cooldown_until') > 0` over each dataset's `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.VIEWS`, and the same over `ops.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.ROUTINES`): **zero hits, in all six.** Repo-wide the string occurs outside this document in exactly two places, neither a predicate anything evaluates at decision time — the `cooldown_until DATE` column line in `bigquery/35_strategy_arsenal.sql`'s `CREATE TABLE … state.strategy_candidates` block, and `bigquery/133_sl1_research_leads_and_record_corrections.sql`'s `AND cooldown_until = DATE '2026-10-25'` assertion guard, a hardcoded-date one-off protecting that file's own G correction. **The gate that would have to honour it does not:** `state.strategy_adoption_readiness` — the `UNDER_REVIEW->SHADOW` gate, whose definition is the highest-numbered `bigquery/*.sql` that CREATEs it (resolve it, do not pin it, per STEP 3's rule; it is `bigquery/143_adversarial_review_correction_path.sql` today) — carries no `cooldown_until` term at all. Its conjuncts are `review_sufficient`, `theater_ok`, `caps_ok`, `ceiling_ok`, `arsenal_ok` and the `':UNDER_REVIEW->SHADOW'` marker test, over `WHERE r.current_state = 'UNDER_REVIEW'`.
     - **LIMB TWO — THE SOLE CONSUMER IS PROSE, AND IT IS LIVE-WORKED, SO THIS IS NOT AN ARGUMENT FROM ABSENCE.** SL1's STEP 3 rail (f) reads "**Cooldown clear** — no same-archetype `cooldown_until` in force (post-rejection / post-termination stamp)", and `strategy/roster.yaml`'s `cooldown_days.post_rejection: 90` says the same in as many words: "a REJECTED candidate / SHADOW-or-PAPER cull cannot re-enter (same archetype) for 90 days". **Candidate H's own `reject_reason`, live in `state.strategy_candidates` today, records that rail being discharged by comparing archetype STRINGS:** "(f) COOLDOWN CLEAR — PASS (archetype distinct from F's defensive-quality-regime-rotation and G's defined-risk-index-downside-convexity; no same-archetype cooldown in force)". That is the entire enforcement mechanism, and it worked — because a session read three strings and compared them.
     - **LIMB THREE — THE GRAIN, WHICH IS THE PART THIS STEP CAN ACTUALLY GET WRONG.** The stamp is pinned `WHERE candidate_code = '<code>'`; the only reader looks for a *same-archetype* cooldown, which it can reach only through `state.strategy_candidates.archetype`. That column is **nullable, and STEP 4 never mentions it** — this STEP names `reject_reason` and `status='REJECTED'` and stops. A culled candidate whose `archetype` is NULL carries a `cooldown_until` that SL1's gate cannot match, so the rail is bypassed silently with a correctly-written stamp sitting in the table. It is reachable today only because the creating routines happened to populate it: F, G and H all carry a non-NULL `archetype` (verified live this fire). **So, while stamping: verify `archetype` is non-NULL on that row, and say so in the `<note>` — the stamp's value is only ever findable through it.**
     - **WHAT SURVIVES IS THE ADDRESS PIN ITSELF, WITH ITS PROPERTY RESTATED.** `state.strategy_candidates` is still the only home for the column and still the only place SL1's reader looks, so a stamp written anywhere else is a stamp nobody finds. What the address buys is **FINDABILITY, not ENFORCEMENT**, and that distinction is the whole of this bullet: the pin's counterfactual ("would leave the cooldown unenforced") has no live contrast to name, because the cooldown is unenforced at the right address too. **Read the stamp as a POLICY RECORD ONLY, NOT MECHANICALLY ENFORCED** — the same marker `strategy/roster.yaml` now carries at `min_shadow_trading_days` and `min_paper_trading_days`, and for the same reason.
     - **DIRECTION, AND IT IS ONLY THE SECOND FINDING IN THIS BLOCK'S HISTORY TO RUN TOWARD THE MONEY.** Every STEP 1 pin — `incubation_day`, `event_ts`, `regime_cell`, the watermark, the grain — fails toward a false REJECT, which the SISA default-REJECT bias absorbs. The 2026-09-24 nesting finding was the first to run the other way; this is the second. A skipped cooldown is a just-culled archetype re-entering incubation early, occupying one of the two `k_incubate` slots and starting a candidate down a pipeline whose end is PROBE and real capital. Nothing absorbs it: there is no alert, no CI check and no view that can observe the rail being ignored.
     - **AND THE REUSABLE HALF, WHICH RHYMES WITH THE 2026-09-06 FINDING AND POINTS THE OPPOSITE WAY.** That sweep found `min_shadow_trading_days` declared in `roster.yaml` and absent from `scripts/check_roster_consistency.py`'s `RAIL_NAMES` / `ROSTER_RAIL_KEY_TO_SQL_NAME`, and concluded that an UNWIRED rail is the hazard. **`reject_cooldown_days` is WIRED and is unenforced anyway:** it IS in `RAIL_NAMES` (alongside `terminate_cooldown_days` and `keep_cooldown_days`), and `ROSTER_COOLDOWN_KEY_TO_SQL_NAME`'s `"post_rejection": "reject_cooldown_days"` entry cross-checks `roster.yaml`'s 90 against the `90 AS reject_cooldown_days` literal in `bigquery/35_strategy_arsenal.sql` and in its `dbt/models/state/arsenal_rails.sql` mirror, on every CI run. So CI proves the NUMBER agrees in three places and nothing anywhere proves the number DOES anything. **A rail can be fully CI-guarded on its value and still have no effect, and that is strictly worse than an unwired one, because the passing check reads as assurance.** Audit what CONSUMES a rail, never only what DECLARES it.
     - **NOT SL3'S TO FIX, and recorded rather than carried silently.** Adding a `cooldown_until` predicate to `state.strategy_adoption_readiness` is live SQL on the gate that admits a candidate to incubation — capital-adjacent, with blast radius across SL1's rail (f) and SL5's branch (1) — and an incubation-monitor fire is the wrong place to decide it unilaterally, the same posture as the 2026-08-25 `regime_met` referral. Raised this run as an `ops.alerts` `info` row (`incubation_cooldown_rail_unenforced_in_sql`) naming `bigquery/143_adversarial_review_correction_path.sql`, `strategy/roster.yaml` and `scripts/check_roster_consistency.py`, after the prescribed open-`info`-row de-dup check found no open row covering it. **One neighbouring fact belongs in that referral rather than in a fix from here: the SQL layer already knows how to enforce a cooldown, and does it for a DIFFERENT one with a literal.** `cooldown_clear` in `state.strategy_retirement_candidacy` (and in the `bigquery/35`, `bigquery/39` and `bigquery/81` generations, plus the `dbt/models/state/strategy_retirement_candidacy.sql` mirror) is a `NOT EXISTS … AND cl.change_ts >= TIMESTAMP_SUB(CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), INTERVAL 90 DAY)` over `ops.roster_change_log` — a hardcoded `INTERVAL 90 DAY` that merely AGREES with `keep_cooldown_days: 90` and reads neither the rail nor `cooldown_until`. That is SL4's surface, named in the referral and untouched here.
     - **Closed BEFORE its first firing**, the same position as every other pin in this block: `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf`, `events.shadow_positions` and `ops.roster_change_log` all hold 0 rows and `state.strategy_roster` carries 0 SHADOW and 0 PAPER members (all re-verified live this fire), so SL3 has never executed a cull and every `cooldown_until` value in the table was stamped by SL1 or A1, not by this STEP. **Prose only — no SQL, no view, no threshold, no gate and no declaration is changed by this revision.** **WHAT FOUND IT, and it is a kind of question neither prescribed instrument asks.** The citation re-audit resolves what a pin POINTS AT, and every object this pin names exists and says what it is said to say; the template census compiles what a template WOULD DO, and STEP 4's `UPDATE` compiles perfectly.    **PAPER TIME-CULL (self-improvement audit ITEM 9, 2026-07-11; WIDENED 2026-08-20 — `bigquery/189_paper_stuck_completeness.sql`).** Read `state.strategy_paper_readiness.stuck` for every PAPER member: if TRUE, this is "stuck", not merely "slow" — write a REJECTED `events.strategy_lifecycle` row + `cooldown_until` stamp exactly like the other two cull paths (never a chat question), and take `reject_reason` from the view's **`stuck_reason`** column rather than composing free text (`stuck_reason IS NOT NULL` ⇔ `stuck`, always — never read the two as independent signals). A candidate whose `declared_annual_roundtrips` genuinely implies a multi-year PAPER window is expected to clear its gates comfortably before 400 days; one that doesn't has either a broken signal-generation path or a materially wrong frequency estimate — either way, sitting indefinitely occupies one of the two `k_incubate` slots another candidate could use.
     - **THE FLAG NOW COVERS ALL THREE PAPER DEAD-ENDS — it previously covered ONE, and the two it missed had NO mechanical cull path anywhere in the system (SL3 2026-08-20 diligence sweep).** `stuck` was `paper_days >= 400 AND NOT trades_met`, interrogating one of the three CANDIDATE-side terms `ready` requires. So a PAPER candidate that traded PLENTY but ran persistently negative on excess-vs-SGOV, or that never demonstrated positive excess in `>=2` regime cells, was `ready = FALSE` (cannot graduate) **and** `stuck = FALSE` (cannot be culled) **forever, at any `paper_days`** — squatting a `k_incubate` slot with nothing to alert on. Two such candidates freeze the entire SISA ADD path: SL1 can synthesize, SL2 can draft, and SL5 can never register another SHADOW member, silently. That is a hard-stop of an autonomous loop, materially worse than the defect ITEM 9 was written to prevent. It is the SAME class as the 2026-07-15 CONFIRMED GAP `shadow-incubation-no-time-cull` on the other axis: STEP 4's OTHER PAPER trigger — "materially-negative paper excess / paper drawdown-kill-equivalent", restated identically at `Experiment_Parameters.md`:209 (repointed from :205 2026-10-19 and from :207 2026-10-25, both by SL3 — rev 20 and rev 21 each inserted two lines above it; the bullet is still struck through) — was unquantified prose with no SQL object, no threshold, no view and no flag behind it, exactly as SHADOW's "window-exceeded" was before `bigquery/60` encoded it. Two documents described that cull; zero objects encoded it, so a session reading `ready=FALSE, stuck=FALSE` correctly did nothing and the condition could persist unnoticed. **`bigquery/60`'s own header says its SHADOW flag "covers all three SHADOW dead-ends" — the mirror was taken in the wrong direction, because the MODEL it copied was the incomplete one.** Now: `stuck = paper_days >= 400 AND NOT (trades_met AND excess_met AND regime_met)`, plus `stuck_reason` naming the failed term(s) (`'trades'` / `'excess'` / `'regime'`, `'+'`-joined). **Arsenal-state terms are DELIBERATELY EXCLUDED and this boundary must not be crossed by a later widening:** `rate_limit_clear`, `ceiling_ok`, `arsenal_ok` and `not_already_transitioned` are conditions of the ARSENAL, not defects of the candidate — a candidate held back only because the roster is at `n_max`, because the 90-day adoption window is closed, or because the owner froze incubation via `ops.arsenal_control` is HEALTHY, and culling it there would let a roster ceiling destroy a qualified candidate and turn the owner's kill-switch from a freeze into a destructive act. `bigquery/60`'s SHADOW `stuck` excludes `arsenal_ok` on exactly this reasoning. The change is a MONOTONE WIDENING (old `stuck` implies new `stuck`), so it can only ever cull more, never rescue something already culled — the fail-safe direction, matching SISA's default-REJECT bias. Closed BEFORE first firing: `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` and `state.strategy_paper_readiness` both hold 0 rows and no strategy has ever entered PAPER, so no live disposition changed; repo and live were verified byte-identical under `scripts/check_live_sql_parity.py`'s own normalization at apply time. **SHADOW TIME-CULL (self-improvement audit 2026-07-15 — CONFIRMED GAP shadow-incubation-no-time-cull, mirrors the PAPER TIME-CULL above exactly).** "Window-exceeded" for SHADOW was previously unquantified prose with no SQL-encoded check. Read `state.strategy_shadow_readiness.stuck` (`bigquery/60_shadow_stuck_cull.sql`; `= shadow_days >= 400 AND NOT (signal_rate_ok AND scaffolding_ok)`) for every SHADOW member: if TRUE, write a REJECTED `events.strategy_lifecycle` row + `cooldown_until` stamp exactly like the other cull paths (never a chat question). This one flag covers all three SHADOW dead-ends (signal floor never clears, signal ceiling breached, scaffolding fault never clears) — SHADOW's own gate only needs 20 trading days + >=1 signal, a much lower bar than PAPER's, so a genuinely healthy candidate should clear `ready` in well under 400 days; one that doesn't has a broken signal-generation path or a persistent scaffolding fault.
     - **AND SHADOW'S `reject_reason` HAS NO SOURCE — THE COLUMN THE SENTENCE ABOVE INVITES A SESSION TO MIRROR IS ONE THE SHADOW VIEW DOES NOT HAVE (SL3 2026-09-06 diligence sweep; the missing-column error reproduced live against BigQuery, not inferred).** STEP 4's general preamble binds EVERY cull path without exception — "Set `reject_reason` and `status='REJECTED'` on the same row while you are there" — and the PAPER TIME-CULL then pins where that value comes from: "take `reject_reason` from the view's **`stuck_reason`** column rather than composing free text". **The SHADOW TIME-CULL names `reject_reason` nowhere at all.** It says to write the REJECTED row and the `cooldown_until` stamp "exactly like the other cull paths" — and those five words ARE the defect, because the thing they invite a session to mirror does not exist on this view: `stuck_reason` is projected by `bigquery/189_paper_stuck_completeness.sql` and its `dbt/models/state/strategy_paper_readiness.sql` mirror and **by nothing else in the repository**. `bigquery/60_shadow_stuck_cull.sql` projects thirteen columns and its dbt mirror the same thirteen; `stuck_reason` is not among them. A repo-wide grep taken BEFORE this revision added its own prose (2026-09-06) returns it in exactly six files: `bigquery/189` (6), `bigquery/35`'s superseded-file comment (1), `dbt/models/state/strategy_paper_readiness.sql` (3), `dbt/models/state/schema.yml`'s description of that same PAPER view (1), `Experiment_Parameters.md`'s PAPER prose (1), and this document's own two PAPER lines with their byte-identical `task_plan/SL3.md` copies — **every reader and every writer of that column is PAPER-specific, without exception.** Given as a FILE LIST rather than a bare total on purpose, per the stability discipline the `alert_message_literal_uncompilable` count in STEP 5 is stated under: prose about this defect — the paragraph you are reading included — can be added without end, and would move a count while changing nothing about the property the count stands for.
       - **THE FAILURE IS A HARD ABORT MID-CULL, AND WHAT IT STRANDS IS THE COOLDOWN.** `SELECT stuck_reason FROM state.strategy_shadow_readiness` returns `Unrecognized name: stuck_reason at [1:8]` — reproduced live this run. That is an ANALYSIS-time error, so the view's 0 rows do not soften it; it fires on the first SHADOW cull ever attempted. If the session happens to select `stuck_reason` alongside `stuck` in its FIRST read, the whole cull aborts before any write and the failure is loud and harmless. **The damaging order is the likelier one**, because it is the order STEP 4 itself prescribes: read `stuck` → write the REJECTED `events.strategy_lifecycle` row → compose the `state.strategy_candidates` `UPDATE` (which is where `reject_reason` is set) → write the `ops.roster_change_log` marker "THEN, AND LAST". The error fires at the third step, so the lifecycle row is already committed and **neither the `UPDATE` nor the marker ever lands**. The member then reads `REJECTED` in `state.strategy_roster`, while `state.strategy_candidates` still carries its pre-cull `status`, no `reject_reason`, and — the field STEP 4 exists to set — **no `cooldown_until`**.
       - **AND NO LATER FIRE REPAIRS IT, WHICH IS WHAT MAKES THIS WORSE THAN A CRASH.** `bigquery/60`'s population filter is `WHERE r.current_state = 'SHADOW'`, so the instant that lifecycle row lands the member drops OUT of `state.strategy_shadow_readiness` altogether; `stuck` can never read TRUE for it again and STEP 4 never revisits it. The missing marker does not rescue this either — the marker's NOT-EXISTS guard is what would let a re-run proceed, but there is nothing left for it to proceed ON. So the end state is permanent and silent: a culled archetype whose post-rejection cooldown was never enforced, free to be re-qualified by SL1 immediately, with nothing to alert on. That is precisely the outcome the 2026-08-19 stamp-address pin was written to prevent, reached by a different route — that pin worried a session would write the stamp to the WRONG table; this one leaves it unwritten entirely.
       - **THE FIX IS THIS BLOCK'S OWN AND IT IS PROSE: derive SHADOW's `reject_reason` from the two booleans `bigquery/60` DOES project, and never query `stuck_reason` on this view.** Compose it as the `'+'`-joined list of failed terms, the same grammar as PAPER's — `'signal_rate'` when `NOT signal_rate_ok`, `'scaffolding'` when `NOT scaffolding_ok`. Note the coarseness deliberately, because it is a genuine difference from PAPER rather than an oversight to "improve" at the write site: `bigquery/60`'s header says its one flag "covers all three SHADOW dead-ends", but `signal_rate_ok` is a single range check **bundling two of them** — the floor never cleared, and the ceiling was breached — which are opposite pathologies (a candidate that never fires; one whose signal path fires spuriously), and `'signal_rate'` alone cannot say which. Where that matters, `signals_generated` and `signal_rate_ceiling` are both projected and separate the two arithmetically: carry them in the `note`, and do NOT invent a third token the PAPER grammar has no counterpart for.
       - **WHY IT SURVIVED, AND THE PART THAT GENERALIZES.** The 2026-08-20 sweep that opened this hole is the same one that widened PAPER's `stuck`: it found SHADOW's flag was the INCOMPLETE model PAPER had been copied from, corrected PAPER in both directions (wider `stuck`, plus the new `stuck_reason`), and wrote the "take it from `stuck_reason`" instruction onto the PAPER path only — leaving the original it had just diagnosed as incomplete no better off, and the SHADOW prose now pointing at a PAPER-only column. **A correction that fixes the copy does not fix the original**, which is the 2026-09-03 sweep's "a correction landed in one routine's section does not sweep its siblings" transposed from two routines to two phases of one routine. No CI check could see it, and for a sharper reason than that sweep's: `scripts/check_handoff_contracts.py`'s `required_column_naming` guards the WRITE side while a readiness view is read-only to SL3 — **and `state.strategy_candidates.reject_reason` is NULLABLE (verified live), so even the write it feeds is invisible to a required-column audit.** **Closed BEFORE its first firing**: `state.strategy_shadow_readiness` holds 0 rows and nothing has ever entered SHADOW, so no cull was ever aborted on this. **Prose only — `bigquery/60`, its dbt mirror, and every gate, threshold and flag are untouched.** Giving the SHADOW view a symmetric `stuck_reason` is the tidier fix and is deliberately NOT taken from an incubation-monitor fire: it must land in `bigquery/60` AND `dbt/models/state/strategy_shadow_readiness.sql` together or turn `main` red on `dbt-parity`, and since SL3 is that view's ONLY reader the derivation above closes the operational hazard completely — no venue is owed for the residual, and none should be manufactured.
       - **ONE METHOD NOTE, because two earlier sweeps in this block leaned on git archaeology.** The origin above is attested by `bigquery/189`'s and `bigquery/60`'s OWN headers, not by commit history. **EVERYTHING THIS NOTE ORIGINALLY SAID ABOUT WHY IT *COULD NOT* BE ATTESTED BY GIT WAS A FACT ABOUT ONE CONTAINER'S CLONE, NOT ABOUT THE REPOSITORY — AND IT IS WITHDRAWN (SL3 2026-09-14 diligence sweep; re-measured live, and the measurement is the point).** It read: "this repository's visible history is 72 commits beginning 2026-08-31, and its ROOT commit already contains `bigquery/35`, `52`, `60` and `189` fully formed, with `189` already carrying `stuck_reason`", and concluded that every in-file date before 2026-08-31 "predates the git record entirely, so `git log -S` can neither confirm nor refute them", ending in the standing instruction **"A future sweep must not cite `git log -S` as evidence for or against a pre-2026-08-31 claim in this repo."** Measured from this run's container: `git rev-parse --is-shallow-repository` returns **`false`**, `git rev-list --count HEAD` returns **1451**, the root commit is `e7be4c0` dated **2026-05-08**, and `git ls-tree -r` on that root returns **ZERO `bigquery/` files** — the directory does not exist there at all. The four files were ADDED later and git says exactly when: `bigquery/35` on 2026-07-10 (`6fb5b69`), `bigquery/52` and `bigquery/60` both on 2026-07-15, and `bigquery/189` on 2026-08-21 (`0776f8f` — the same commit SL3's own OPS2 STEP 0 worked example names). **So every load-bearing clause was wrong: not 72 commits but 1451, not beginning 2026-08-31 but 2026-05-08, and the root commit contains none of the four rather than all of them.** The cited `2026-07-15` and `2026-08-20` audit dates are INSIDE the git record, not outside it. **THE DIAGNOSIS, which is the reusable part and is not "the numbers aged":** a shallow clone's `git rev-list --count` and its oldest commit describe **the checkout the session happens to be holding**, and this note promoted both into properties of the repository — the precise confusion this document already has a rule for, the **HISTORY-DEPTH PRECHECK** (§Execution environment), whose entire purpose is to distinguish "history absent" from "history not fetched". The block was holding both readings at once and never reconciled them: the 2026-09-06 note asserted a 72-commit repository while the STEP 4 `roster.yaml` bullet, written **two days later**, already recorded a `git log -S` attribution "re-verified 2026-09-08 against a fully-deepened clone". **The withdrawn instruction was the expensive half**, because it forbids a measurement that is available and better than the file-header attestation it deferred to — a sweep obeying it would decline to check the one thing that settles these claims. **Replacing it: run the HISTORY-DEPTH PRECHECK, and if the clone is not shallow, `git log -S` IS admissible evidence back to 2026-05-08; only report a negative as "not found in the fetched history" when the precheck says the clone is shallow and cannot be deepened.** **What SURVIVES, now on stronger footing rather than weaker:** across the full 1451-commit history nothing has ever added `stuck_reason` to `bigquery/60` or its dbt mirror (`git log -S` over both paths returns no commit), so the asymmetry has been constant for the file's entire real life — a claim the 72-commit window could only make about three weeks of it. The 2026-08-24 `cooldown_until` correction remains the cautionary case for the DIFFERENT failure it actually illustrates: reasoning about the WORLD from what a FILE said instead of querying it.

The dropped one is `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf`, registered in `ops/handoff_contracts.yaml`'s `write_targets` section as its `- table: analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` entry, whose `why:` cites SL3's own `incubation_day` WRITE CONTRACT pin by name — so the omission understated the very argument this sentence is making, and it dropped the table carrying the most load-bearing required-column pin in this block out of the list offered as its evidence**. **THAT CITATION READ `ops/handoff_contracts.yaml`:124-125 AND HAD DRIFTED WITHIN NINE DAYS — converted to the named anchor above 2026-09-10 (SL3 diligence sweep, which re-resolved every `path:NNN` pin in this block against the tree).** The entry now sits at lines 141-142, and the drift earns more than a renumber for two reasons. **First, it is the shortest-lived pin this block has produced:** it was written by SL3's OWN 2026-09-01 correction — the parenthetical you are reading — and was wrong nine days later, against a file this block neither owns nor paces. **Second, WHERE it landed is the hazard, not how far it moved:** lines 124-125 are not neutral filler but a live clause of that file's own EXCLUSION rationale, asserting that an object is an "owner-only control-plane toggle" with "no INSERT/UPDATE instruction … anywhere in Claude_Task_Plan.md" — that is, a stated reason a table is NOT a registered write target. A session following the pin to confirm that `analytics.strategy_incubation_perf` IS registered would land on text arguing the opposite proposition and could reasonably conclude the registration had been withdrawn. That is this block's own rule — "a drifted pin is worse than no pin — it points at real code that is not the code meant" — in its most expensive form: the wrong target does not merely fail to support the claim, it appears to refute it. **The 2026-08-23 remedy generalizes past `bigquery/35`:** it converted pins to anchors for files "that keep accreting long inline notes above the constructs cited here", and `ops/handoff_contracts.yaml` — whose header commentary alone runs past line 130 and grows with every allowlist rationale added — is exactly that kind of file. **Re-measured this run, and recorded so the next sweep can skip it:** this was the ONE pin in the block aimed at a YAML spec, and it is the one that moved; every surviving `:NNN` pin points into `bigquery/01_schema.sql`, `bigquery/52`, `bigquery/60`, `bigquery/62` or `Experiment_Parameters.md`, and all of them re-resolved to the constructs they claim) — and `scripts/check_handoff_contracts.py`'s `required_column_naming` check verifies every `NOT NULL`-with-no-default column of each is named somewhere in this document — so the required-column audits that produced the STEP 1/3/4 pins ran against a COMPLETE list even while the summary declarations drifted. **Nothing anywhere validates `ops/cadence.yaml`'s `writes:` list, or the ROUTINE INVENTORY's reads/writes cells, against the routine's own steps** — `scripts/check_cadence_consistency.py` parses that table's `Cadence · Type` cell ONLY (its own docstring says so, and its parser extracts no other column), and `ops/handoff_contracts.yaml`'s `write_targets` is a hand-curated enumeration by that file's own scope comment, not derived from `cadence.yaml` — which is the mechanism that let this survive seven weeks. That gap is NOT closed from an SL3 fire: a check of that kind is a fleet-wide CI change that would first have to be validated against all **33** routines' declarations (the count `scripts/check_cadence_consistency.py` itself reports — **corrected from 32 on 2026-09-14 (SL3 diligence sweep), by RUNNING the script rather than quoting a remembered figure: it prints `CADENCE CONSISTENCY: OK — 33 routines`, and `ops/cadence.yaml` carries 33 `- id:` entries. The figure was cited three times in this block against a source that reports it on every CI run, while the SAME STEP already said "measured across all 33 `writes:` cells of `ops/cadence.yaml`" — so the block contradicted itself about one measured quantity, in the direction that UNDERSTATES the survey it is scoping. A count borrowed from a script is only as current as the last time someone ran the script**), and turning `main` red on landed history is the failure CLAUDE.md warns about by name. Recorded as an `events.decision_log` `ops-note` (2026-08-30) and referred to W5 / the owner, the same posture as the 2026-08-25 `regime_met` and `roster.yaml`-staleness referrals. **ADJUDICATED 2026-08-30 BY W5 — ACCEPTED AS REAL and independently corroborated (it enumerated all 19 `scripts/check_*.py` and found no checker that validates a declared surface), NOT FIXED, and SL3's sequencing explicitly ENDORSED (recorded here 2026-08-31).** W5's decision: **a blocking CI check must NOT be landed first**, for exactly the reason stated above; the sequenced path of record is **(i)** survey all 33 routines, diffing `ops/cadence.yaml` `writes:` and the INVENTORY cells against the tables each routine's own section names, **(ii)** land the corrections routine by routine, **(iii)** add the check LAST. It also endorsed narrowing the eventual check to the **WRITES side only** — the reads cells read as abbreviated summaries across the fleet and several are visibly partial, while writes completeness is load-bearing for handoff contracts, DR rebuild and the required-column audits. Not started; a 32-routine survey is its own unit of work and is NOT an SL3 errand. **SL3's own two declarations were re-verified clean this run** (`ops/cadence.yaml`'s `writes:` list and the ROUTINE INVENTORY row both carry the eight true DML surfaces and correctly omit the coverage VIEW; `ops.run_log` is absent from both — that is fleet convention, not SL3 drift). **BUT THE REASON THIS CLAUSE GAVE FOR THAT LAST ABSENCE WAS WRONG ON BOTH HALVES, AND APPLIED AS WRITTEN IT PREDICTS THE OPPOSITE OF THE DECLARATION IT IS DEFENDING (SL3 2026-09-13 diligence sweep; measured across all 33 `writes:` cells of `ops/cadence.yaml`, not inferred).** Until this revision it read that `ops.run_log` is absent "as it is from every routine's cell, because it is procedure-wrapped". **Procedure-wrapping is not the discriminator, and the fleet says so 26 times over:** `events.decision_log` is procedure-wrapped behind `ops.sp_log_decision` and is declared in **18** of the 33 `writes:` cells; `ops.alerts` is procedure-wrapped behind `ops.sp_raise_alert`/`sp_raise_alert_once` and is declared in **8** — and **SL3's own eight-surface list carries BOTH of them.** So the stated rule, followed literally, yields a SIX-surface SL3 writes list and licenses "correcting" both declarations down from eight — the exact direction the 2026-08-30 sweep corrected them AWAY from, on the very pair of surfaces that sweep had just finished repairing, and on a file `scripts/check_handoff_contracts.py` reads. **And the universal is false outright: `ops.run_log` appears in exactly ONE routine's `writes:` cell — OPS2's** (`ops/cadence.yaml`, its `- id: OPS2` entry, alongside `ops.catchup_refire_log`) — **and legitimately**, because OPS2 writes run-log rows ON BEHALF OF the routines it re-fires, which is a genuine DML write and not its own run-logging protocol. **The real convention is narrower than either half, and is stated here once: a routine does not declare the run-log rows it writes ABOUT ITSELF as part of run-logging, and OPS2 is not an exception to that rule but a writer of a different kind of row.**
     - **WHY THE WRONG REASON WAS LOAD-BEARING RATHER THAN DECORATIVE — it is the only thing this STEP offers to reconcile its own two counts of SL3's write surfaces, and it reconciles them backwards.** This clause says **eight**; the required-column re-verification below says "every required column of all **six** surfaces SL3 writes". Both are correct, under different predicates — eight is the DECLARED DML surface set, six is the subset reached by DIRECT DML, `events.decision_log` and `ops.alerts` dropping out because a routine reaches them only through `sp_log_decision` / `sp_raise_alert_once`, whose ARGUMENT lists, not the tables' columns, are what a caller must satisfy. **That predicate is not unwritten — it is `ops/handoff_contracts.yaml`'s `procedure_wrapped:` section, which registers `ops.run_log`, `ops.alerts`, `events.decision_log` and `events.adversarial_reviews` one table per entry with a per-table `why:`, and which is exactly what scopes `scripts/check_handoff_contracts.py`'s `required_column_naming` check — but it appears nowhere in THIS block, and the reason that did appear points a reader at the six and calls the eight drift.** **That is the precise diagnosis, and it is sharper than "the reason was wrong": the clause reached for a REAL rule and applied it to the WRONG LIST.** Procedure-wrapping genuinely does govern a list — the required-column audit's exclusions, which is precisely the operation that turns the eight into the six — and it governs `ops/cadence.yaml`'s `writes:` declaration not at all, which is why two procedure-wrapped surfaces sit in SL3's own eight and why `ops.run_log` sits in OPS2's. Stated here so the next required-column audit need not guess: **the six-list is that audit's SCOPE — the eight minus the `procedure_wrapped` registrations — not a competing account of what SL3 writes, and the eight-list is not to be trimmed to match it.** Note the shape, which is this block's own recurring one arriving from a new direction: the 2026-09-01 and 2026-09-10 corrections to this same STEP each repaired a CITATION that pointed at the wrong place, whereas this one is a stated REASON that names a real and correctly-documented rule and still licenses the wrong edit, by carrying it across a list boundary. **The citation-audit reflex does not catch it, because there is no citation to resolve** — every object it names exists and says what it is said to say. **Prose only — no SQL, no view, no threshold and no gate is changed, and no declaration is edited: both of SL3's were re-measured correct this run.** The two counts are stated as measured totals over a file other routines own and will move with the fleet; what does not move, and is the reason this is written down, is the PROPERTY — procedure-wrapping alone has never determined whether a surface is declared.

## H-SL3-07 — BEFORE WRITING THE HEARTBEAT, RERESOLVE SL3'S OWN OPEN REFERRALS — THE REFERRAL LOOP HAS NO RETURN PATH, AND T

A routine's own section is the text it executes from; a stale referral there is not a documentation blemish, it is an instruction to do the wrong thing. **So, each fire, before STEP 5's heartbeat: for every referral this block still describes as open, read `state.decision_log_current` for a later `ops-note` / `routine-outcome` row adjudicating it (W5's spec-defect notice intake is the usual venue), and re-measure any live claim the referral rests on — an `ops.alert_policy` row, a view's rows, a `bigquery/NNN` file's existence. ## H-SL3-08 — THE CATCHUP ITSELF IS THE FIRST THING WORTH RECORDING, BECAUSE THE MACHINERY IN STEP 1 HAD NEVER ONCE RUN WIT

_Moved verbatim from `Claude_Task_Plan.md` (SL3, was line 5352) on 2026-10-30. Historical record only; the binding rule is the condensed text in the plan._

     - **THE CATCH-UP ITSELF IS THE FIRST THING WORTH RECORDING, BECAUSE THE MACHINERY IN STEP 1 HAD NEVER ONCE RUN WITH N > 1.** Every prior fire in this series measured `state.routine_catchup_window` at `window_days` 0.98-0.99 and wrote “cadence-normal, NO CATCHUP token owed”. This fire measured **3.99**, so the token **`CATCHUP[window_days=3.99]`** is owed and appears in the completion `<note>` per the shared CATCH-UP EVIDENCE WINDOW telemetry rule (>1.5x the routine’s own cadence). The cause was a fleet-wide outage, not an SL3 defect: `ops.run_log` carries **no row of any routine** between 2026-09-17 23:15 MT and 2026-09-20 01:38 MT, so SL3’s 2026-09-17 evening slot and its 09-18/09-19 non-slots all fell inside it. The day set was derived, not assumed, and is **{2026-09-17, 2026-09-18}** — the two days between SL3’s last completion (2026-09-16) and today’s `last_trading_day` (2026-09-18, Friday; today 2026-09-20 is a Sunday and `is_trading_day` FALSE) that carry a `state.daily_marks_curated` row. Both resolved a complete `regime_cell` as-of from `events.regime_events` with the `(event_ts DESC, event_id DESC)` tiebreaker — **2026-09-17 = `UP/NORMAL`, 2026-09-18 = `UP/LOW`** — and the fact that the two days resolve to DIFFERENT cells is the first live demonstration that STEP 1’s as-of `regime_cell` pin (2026-08-27) does real work: a session that had reached for `state.current_regime` would have stamped one cell onto both.
     - **AND THE NULL-FALLBACK’S STATED PRECONDITION WAS LIVE-TESTABLE FOR THE FIRST TIME AND DID NOT OCCUR — recorded as a measurement, NOT as a refutation.** That bullet justifies its fail-safe NULL by arguing the case is “genuinely reachable” because “D2a’s regime write is a separate step from its marks ingest, so a day can in principle carry marks and no regime pair”. 2026-09-18 is the sharpest possible test of it: **D2a has no `ops.run_log` row of any status for that date at all**, so every 09-18 row in both tables was written by D2a’s own catch-up on 2026-09-20. Measured, the two legs moved TOGETHER rather than apart — marks first written 09-20 16:47 MT, the regime pair 09-20 16:56 MT, nine minutes later in the same D2a fire. So on the one occasion the hazard could have fired it did not, and the fallback stays unexercised. **Do NOT read this as licence to drop the fallback**: one observation of two legs co-occurring in one catch-up is not a guarantee they cannot diverge, the fallback costs nothing when unexercised, and its failure direction (understating breadth) is the SISA default-REJECT one. It is recorded so the next sweep knows the claim has been tested once, and how.
     - **AN OPEN `catchup_refire_blocked` ALERT NAMES THIS RUN, AND SL3 MUST NOT RESOLVE IT BY HAND.** `ops.alerts` `3f82b742` (warning, raised 2026-09-20 18:56 MT) carries `miss_key = SL3|2026-09-17` and asks an operator to “manually re-run routine SL3 for 2026-09-17”. The 08-20 correction reasoned only about SL1 masking SL3, and that direction is the WEAKER one: an SL1 row is monthly (quarterly before rev 21), so it can only mask a missing SL3 heartbeat in the rare window where an SL1 row happens to be the freshest. **The direction it missed is continuous and cannot self-clear.** W5 asserts "SL1 in the last month (quarter before rev 21), SL3 in the last trading day, and SL4 in the last month" against ONE `entry_type`, and SL3 writes DAILY — so a check keyed on `entry_type` alone would find a row inside every one of those windows on every run, permanently, from SL3's own rows. **A totally dead SL1 or SL4 would never be detected, and the SL3 leg is the ONLY one of the three that its own daily cadence does not silently satisfy.** The prescription is unchanged and was already right — `entry_type='arsenal-heartbeat'` AND `title LIKE 'SL3 %'`, and by extension `'SL1 %'` / `'SL4 %'` for the sibling legs — it is the evidence and the stated blast radius that were understated; The remedy they prescribe for the sibling legs manufactures the opposite failure and a worse-behaved one: a check encoded as `title LIKE 'SL1 %'` against the current view finds **no SL1 heartbeat at any date, ever**, and fires a permanent `arsenal_heartbeat_missing` on a perfectly healthy quarterly routine. **It cannot self-clear** — the next SL1 heartbeat that gets corrected sheds the prefix the same way — so it is a LATCHING false alarm, not a transient one, on a category that (see the deadman bullet below) is registered nowhere in `ops.alert_policy`.

This is not a note about SL1's leg; it is a live exposure on SL3's, and SL3 corrects its own record often enough that the case is reachable rather than exotic.

re-measured live against `ops.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.PARAMETERS` this run — takes **five** positional arguments and the third is `in_category`. **Enumerated across this whole document there are 22 `sp_raise_alert` call sites at `'info'` severity and 21 of them supply a literal category** — the shared Observability template's one, D3's three, OPS0's four, OPS2's one, W5's eight, Q4's two, A3's one and SL2's three-way disjunction — **and this one was the sole exception.** It does not fail loudly either: `ops.alerts.category` is NULLABLE (`bigquery/10_observability.sql`'s `CREATE TABLE … ops.alerts` block), so the elided form inserts a row with no category at all rather than raising. **THE SIBLING THAT MAKES THIS MORE THAN A TYPOGRAPHIC GAP IS SL2, AND IT HAS ALREADY PAID FOR IT.** SL2 is the same family, the same `'info'` severity, the same non-`_once` procedure and the same kind of notice — a completed, healthy, fully-autonomous lifecycle action needing no owner response — and its call pins three literals outright (`'strategy_drafted'|'strategy_revised'|'postmortem_written'`, in SL2's own section, mirrored in `bigquery/185_sl2_notice_alert_lifecycle.sql`'s header). A row carrying NULL, or carrying whichever string two different sessions each invented, is an audit record unfindable by the one field anything looks it up with. **Same shape as the `PENDING_ROSTER` lane string (pinned 2026-08-18) and the `'<code>:SHADOW->PAPER'` marker (2026-08-19) — a cross-session identifier that the write site's own text never named — and the third instance of it on this routine's own two transitions.** **On the two literals chosen:** one per event class, because a single category could not tell a promotion from a cull and the cull is the destructive one; both follow the `strategy_`-prefixed past-tense form six of the seven sibling notice categories use; and both are free — re-measured this run against every row of `ops.alert_policy` and every `DISTINCT category` ever written to `ops.alerts`, where neither string appears and the only `promot`-bearing categories are D3's `monitor_promoted` and W5's `loop_promoted`. (The running registered-category TOTAL stays retired per the 2026-09-07 decision; what is measured here is the collision property, not a count.) **`strategy_graduated` is the one they could be confused with and is NOT this** — it is M4's `PROBE->ADOPTED` at the 30-trade gate, three states later — and the house has already been bitten by exactly that confusion once, which is why `strategy_adopted` was renamed to `strategy_probe_registered` on 2026-08-04 (`bigquery/134`'s own policy note records the rename and its reason). Both messages are deliberately **apostrophe-free**, the shared rules' PREFERRED route past the `''`/`\'` trap rather than the escape — the same route OPS1's repair took — and both keep every varying quantity OUT of the message and in the payload, per the ROSTER-CHANGE NOTICES payload rule ("the payload is enrichment and the message is not"). The payload is `TO_JSON_STRING(<row>)` and never `TO_JSON(<row>)`, for the reason the sibling call two sentences on already records: `in_payload_json` is a `STRING` and `SAFE.PARSE_JSON` stores NULL rather than erroring on anything else. **`sp_raise_alert`, not `sp_raise_alert_once`, is correct here and must NOT be "reconciled" with that probe-stall call:** STEPs 3 and 4 each NOT-EXISTS-guard on their `ops.roster_change_log` `change_key` BEFORE the branch runs, so the notice inherits that idempotency and a second session never reaches the `CALL` at all. **REGISTRATION IS NOT SL3'S, AND IS RECORDED RATHER THAN CARRIED SILENTLY.** With the literals now named, both categories are absent from `ops.alert_policy` and so would latch on first firing with no mechanical closer — `ops.sp_auto_resolve_alerts`'s rules are hardcoded to `missing_dependency` / `missed_run` / `routine_stalled` / `catchup_refire_blocked` / `staleness` plus the roster-notice on-delivery rule, and none of them reaches an `info` row that is never delivered. So the dedup predicate can never match a prior day's row, `sp_raise_alert_once` degenerates to a plain `sp_raise_alert`, and the stall raises a NEW alert every day it persists — the precise pile-up the call was chosen to prevent, on a condition whose only remedy is an owner deposit that may legitimately take days or weeks. **Fix, and it is entirely within this line's gift: keep the varying integers OUT of the `<message>` and carry them in the payload, where they already belong.** Raise a STABLE message — `CONCAT(strategy_code, ' is frozen below the $2,000 probe floor — deposit or sunset-redistribution routing needed; the deposit is the sanctioned owner touch, this alert is the in-band surfacing bigquery/62\'s header promised but never wired')` — and put `days_since_probe_entry` / `funding_gap_dollars` / `booked_allocation` in the payload argument. This is the ROSTER-CHANGE NOTICES payload rule applied here ("the payload is enrichment and the message is not; the `<message>` argument MUST stand alone as a complete sentence"), and with a stable message the dedup works exactly as this line always claimed: one open alert per stalled strategy, not one per stalled strategy per day. **Also pin the payload's CONSTRUCTION, not just its content:** `in_payload_json` is a **`STRING`**, not BigQuery's native `JSON` type, and `sp_raise_alert` stores it via `SAFE.PARSE_JSON(in_payload_json)` — so pass `TO_JSON_STRING(<row>)` (the construction `ops.sp_log_decision`'s own body uses for this identical argument position). So the obligation is SL3's, on every fire, and it is this: **for any `strategy_code` carrying an OPEN `probe_funding_stalled` alert that NO LONGER appears in `state.strategy_probe_funding_stalled`** — the funding gap closed, the owner deposit landed, or the probe was withdrawn — resolve it, `UPDATE ops.alerts SET resolved=TRUE, resolved_ts=CURRENT_TIMESTAMP(), resolved_note='<strategy_code>; <what closed the gap>; SL3 <run_date>' WHERE alert_id='<id>' AND NOT resolved`. **SCOPE BY `alert_id`**, against the one `strategy_code` just re-verified — never a blanket clear of the category, since two strategies can stall independently — and **NEVER resolve on age**: an aged-out row is indistinguishable from a genuinely-funded one, and the whole condition is a newcomer sitting below its stake floor waiting for capital that may simply never have arrived, which is the aging-alert trap in the place it would cost most. **AND THE DUTY'S IDENTIFICATION HALF HAS NO TEMPLATE, WHILE THE SPELLING A SESSION REACHES FOR DOES NOT COMPILE (SL3 2026-09-27 diligence sweep; reproduced against BigQuery while EXECUTING this duty, not inferred).** The sentence above supplies the `UPDATE` and its `resolved_note` format and then leaves `<id>` to the session — but `ops.alerts` carries no `strategy_code` column (its columns are `alert_id`, `alert_ts`, `severity`, `source`, `category`, `message`, `payload` (`JSON`), `resolved`, `resolved_ts`, `resolved_note` and `notified_ts`), so "the `strategy_code` carrying an OPEN alert that no longer appears in the view" has to be recovered from the row before anything can be compared. The natural form — a correlated `NOT EXISTS` matching the code inside `message` — is rejected at analysis time: **`LEFT ANTISEMI JOIN cannot be used without a condition that is an equality of fields from both sides of the join`**; BigQuery will not accept a `STRPOS`-style predicate as the correlation. **The compiling form takes the code out of the PAYLOAD, where the raise leg one clause above already put it via `TO_JSON_STRING(r)`, and joins on equality:** `SELECT a.alert_id FROM ops.alerts a LEFT JOIN state.strategy_probe_funding_stalled s ON s.strategy_code = JSON_VALUE(a.payload, '$.strategy_code') WHERE a.category = 'probe_funding_stalled' AND NOT a.resolved AND s.strategy_code IS NULL` — run this fire, returning zero rows as it must against an empty source view. The failure is LOUD and pre-write, so nothing can be silently mis-resolved by it, which is why this is a spelling pin and not a defect record. It is a further instance of the 2026-09-16 blind spot, arriving on the one leg of this duty that carried no template at all, and **the block-wide template census cannot see it by construction: the census audits the templates a block GIVES, and it enumerated this duty as the `UPDATE` alone, treating `<id>` as supplied.** A missing template is invisible to an instrument that enumerates templates. **Extend the census: wherever a template's arguments include a placeholder the block does not itself compute, enumerate the QUERY that computes it as a template of its own, and compile that too.**
