     - **AND THE MESSAGE LITERAL ITSELF DID NOT COMPILE — `''` IS NOT AN ESCAPE IN GOOGLESQL, AND THIS DOCUMENT'S OWN SHARED CONVENTIONS ALREADY SAID SO (SL3 2026-09-01 diligence sweep; reproduced against BigQuery, not inferred).** BOTH spellings of this line's `sp_raise_alert_once` message — the original template above and the STABLE replacement pinned 2026-08-26 — wrote the possessive as `bigquery/62''s`. That is the ANSI/Postgres/Oracle idiom for an escaped apostrophe and it is **invalid in GoogleSQL**, which reads `'…62''s…'` as two adjacent string literals and rejects it: `Syntax error: concatenated string literals must be separated by whitespace or comments`. Reproduced this run against the exact literal, and the corrected text then re-run as a complete `CALL … sp_raise_alert_once(…, TO_JSON_STRING(<row>))` dry-run to confirm it parses end-to-end. **Both spellings are corrected to `bigquery/62\'s`** (a `"…"` or `r'''…'''` literal would serve equally, and is the better choice if this message ever gains a second apostrophe). Three things make it more than a typo. **(i) It is the SAME failure mode as the `TO_JSON(...)` trap pinned in the sentence immediately above** — the `CALL` does not compile on the FIRST stall, which is the only occasion this line ever executes — so the 2026-08-26 revision, whose entire subject was the compile-safety of this one call, hardened the payload argument while re-typing the identical class of defect into the message argument it was replacing. **A pin can carry a defect into the very text that closes one**, and the audit that produced it will not catch that, because it was looking at the argument it had already decided was the problem. **(ii) The rule was already written down and this block broke it anyway.** The shared file-write conventions in this document state **"NEVER double a `'` into `''`. GoogleSQL does not use SQL-92 quote doubling"**, and record that BOTH its failure modes — this syntax error, and a *silent* stored doubled-apostrophe inside a triple-quoted literal — actually occurred on 2026-08-04. So the hazard was known, named, and dated in the same file; what failed is the assumption that a stated convention reaches a literal typed 3,600 lines away. **(iii) Nothing live changed** — the category has never been raised (0 rows in `ops.alerts`, 0 in `state.strategy_probe_funding_stalled`, 0 strategies have ever reached PROBE) — so this is closed before its first firing, the same position as the `SHADOW->PAPER` marker, the `->REJECTED` marker and the `regime_cell` pins. **THE FLEET-WIDE HALF IS NOT CLOSED FROM AN SL3 FIRE, and is explicitly not SL3's text to edit:** when this was written the identical defect stood in FOUR other routines' alert literals — **D3** (two lines, three occurrences), **OPS1**, **Q4** and **A3**, eight executable occurrences across five lines — and three of those (D3, Q4, A3) additionally carried a **bare, undoubled** `'` inside the same single-quoted message (`old_value is the field's value`, `D3's next daily run`, `Q4's quarterly backstop`), which terminates the literal earlier still and is the louder half of the same defect. All eight sites and the bare-apostrophe sites were enumerated and classified that run against the legitimate `COALESCE(x, '')` empty-string uses and the `r'''…'''` triple-quote delimiters, so the finding was a measured list and not a grep count. **RE-MEASURED 2026-09-03 (SL3), AND IT HAS MOVED — D3 REPAIRED ITS OWN SITES: five executable occurrences remained at that date, across three lines in THREE routine blocks — `OPS1` (one), `Q4` (two), `A3` (two) — of which `Q4` and `A3` still also carried the bare undoubled apostrophe.** D3 fixed both of its lines on 2026-09-02 and left a `do NOT revert` pin in its own block; its three occurrences are gone. **CLOSED 2026-09-06/07 — W5 REPAIRED THE REMAINING THREE SITES AND DRAINED THE ROW; RE-MEASURED TO ZERO 2026-09-07 (SL3).** W5's SPEC-DEFECT NOTICE INTAKE on its 2026-09-06 fire corrected every remaining site and resolved the `info` row (`93058867`) at 23:32 MT, its `resolved_note` recording that "Both the doubled-quote AND the bare-apostrophe class were fixed at each site", verified by a 144-call-site re-sweep and by dry-run compiling all three corrected literals against BigQuery. **Independently re-measured this run by CLASSIFYING every `''` line in the file rather than counting matches — the same method this block has used throughout: 20 lines carry `''`, and every one of them is an `r'''…'''` delimiter, a `COALESCE(x, '')`-class empty string, or prose about this defect. ZERO executable occurrences fleet-wide, and zero bare undoubled apostrophes at the three sites.** `OPS1`'s message was closed by the shared rules' *preferred* route — reworded so it needs no apostrophe at all — while `Q4` (four occurrences) and `A3` (five) now carry `\'` at every one, each under its own `do NOT revert` ALERT-LITERAL QUOTING pin; the `task_plan/` slices are byte-identical to the monolith at all three sites plus this one. **The per-site counts W5 repaired are LARGER than the two-and-two this block recorded, and that is a reconciliation rather than a correction of either side:** this block counted the doubled-quote class only, W5 counted that class together with the bare undoubled apostrophes inside the same literals, and each count was right about its own predicate. The pre-repair state is no longer measurable from the tree, so it is recorded as a reconciliation and not re-derived. **This count is stated over EXECUTABLE occurrences only — a `''` inside a literal a routine is instructed to run — deliberately excluding the prose occurrences in which this document discusses the defect, including the ones in this very sentence and their byte-identical copies in `task_plan/`.** That is what makes it a stable measurement rather than the self-perturbing kind SL5's STEP 0 generated-duplicate rule warns about: prose about the defect can be added without end and cannot move this number. The `ops.alerts` `info` row stayed correctly OPEN through the partial D3 repair — a repair by one of the named owners is not a drain while the majority of the sites it names are outstanding — and was then drained in full by the owner venue it was addressed to, which is the OUT-OF-SCOPE FINDINGS rule working end to end: SL3 measured and recorded, W5 owned and fixed. Recorded as an `ops.alerts` `info` row (`alert_message_literal_uncompilable`) naming every site and the owning routine block, per the 2026-08-28 OUT-OF-SCOPE FINDINGS rule; rewriting four other routines' executable alert text from an incubation-monitor fire is exactly the scope violation this block declines everywhere else.
     - **STEP 5'S HEARTBEAT WRITE — THE `decision` SLOT HAS FRAGMENTED INTO THREE SPELLINGS ACROSS 52 FIRES, TWO OF THEM COINED VARIANTS THE SHARED RULES FORBID BY NAME (SL3 2026-09-16 diligence sweep; every SL3 heartbeat row on record CLASSIFIED, not sampled).** `decision` is one of this system's two controlled vocabularies — §Decision-log lifecycle's write-discipline list, its `entry_type` and `decision` are CONTROLLED VOCABULARIES bullet ("Do not coin a variant"), with the vocabulary given two paragraphs on as "`GO` / `NO-GO` / `DEFER` / `HOLD` / `NO-ACTION` plus the exit family". Measured against `state.decision_log_current` through this STEP's own tolerant filter, SL3's 52 `arsenal-heartbeat` rows carry **FOUR** different treatments of that one slot: **`no_transition` (6 rows, 2026-07-10..07-30), `EVALUATED_NO_CHANGE` (15, 2026-08-04..08-23), `HOLD` (1, 2026-08-24) and NULL (30, from 2026-07-11 and unbroken across the last sixteen fires since 2026-08-25)**. **Only `HOLD` is in the vocabulary, and it is the one used once.** The other fifteen slots of the same call show no such exposure, and that contrast is the diagnosis rather than a reassurance: `strategy`, `ticker`, `conviction`, `conviction_pct`, `sub_pattern` and `theater_check` are NULL on all 52 — perfectly consistent — while `fields`, `refs` and `tags` vary freely and are MEANT to. **`decision` is the only one of the sixteen slots where variation is forbidden, and it is the only one that varied.**
       - **WHY IT DRIFTED — AND IT IS *NOT* "THIS BLOCK GIVES NO `CALL` TEMPLATE", A HYPOTHESIS TESTED AND FALSIFIED THIS RUN.** The obvious reading is that the heartbeat instruction names the table and the `entry_type` and stops, while `ops.sp_log_decision` takes **sixteen** positional arguments (re-measured live against `ops.INFORMATION_SCHEMA.PARAMETERS`), leaving each session to fill the other fifteen from nothing. But eliding is the FLEET NORM rather than an SL3 omission: of the **88** `sp_log_decision(` call sites in this document, **86 write `(...)`** and exactly two spell an argument list out (M4's `premortem-indicator-walk`, D1's `add-candidate-review`), and the shared preamble gives no template either. A convention 86 sites follow cannot be what singles this one out. **The actual discriminator is WHICH FIELDS this block has ever named, and it maps onto the drift exactly.** Four sweeps hardened this one write — 2026-08-19 (`entry_type` plus the `title` prefix, not `tags`), 2026-08-20 (the two are a CONJUNCTION), 2026-09-07 (the `entry_type` is three-way shared), 2026-09-09 (the filter must tolerate `CORRECTED — ` and be read from the current view) — and all four are about `entry_type` and `title`, for one stated reason: W5's meta-heartbeat check READS those two. **Every field a consumer keys on is pinned to the character; every field no consumer reads was never mentioned once — and that is precisely the set that drifted.** So the rule is not "pin the call"; it is that **a write's UN-READ fields are exactly where drift is invisible, and an audit scoped to what a consumer reads can never find it.**
       - **THE PRECEDENT SITS IN THE SAME RULE LIST AND IT SAYS THIS IS LUCK, NOT SAFETY.** §Decision-log lifecycle pins the exit family at the single token `exit` after measuring **six spellings of one concept**, and says outright that the only reason that fragmentation stayed harmless is that "no live view or procedure filters on the exit family at all" — "That is luck, not design: the moment anyone writes an exit-side learning view, five of the six spellings silently vanish from it." The same sentence transfers here verbatim. Nothing filters on `decision` for `arsenal-heartbeat` today; the two mechanical backstops that rule list names do not reach it either, since the GO filters are a `^GO\b`-anchored family test that none of these four values would match and the `analytics.thesis_outcomes` tolerance is for the `thesis` `entry_type` synonym; and **no `scripts/check_*.py` validates the `decision` vocabulary at all** (repo-wide grep this run — the rule is prose-enforced only, as that same list already concedes when it says a NEW variant "is still invisible to both"). The exposure is a future arsenal-side learning view, and the failure it would produce — three of four cohorts silently dropped — is the `thesis` / `thesis-construction` incident the rule list opens with, which cost a real Strategy B GO its place in every calibration view and "was never noticed".
       - **THE FIX IS PROSE AND IT IS THIS BLOCK'S OWN: write `decision = NULL` on the `arsenal-heartbeat` row, deliberately, and it is stated at the instruction above so the next session does not have to choose.** NULL is not a dodge, and it is not merely what the last sixteen fires happened to do — it is the semantically correct value. **A heartbeat asserts that an EVALUATION OCCURRED, which is not a decision.** Every decision SL3 actually takes is a lifecycle transition and is recorded where transitions are recorded: `events.strategy_lifecycle` plus the `ops.roster_change_log` marker (STEPs 3 and 4), surfaced by its own `info` notice (`strategy_paper_promoted` / `strategy_incubation_culled`, pinned 2026-09-15). The heartbeat is never the venue for one — on a quiescent fire or a transitioning one — so no token belongs in the slot, and **specifically no mapping from "transitions fired" onto `GO`/`NO-GO` should be invented for it: coining a MAPPING is the same defect as coining a TOKEN, one level up.** The in-vocabulary candidate a later session will reach for is `NO-ACTION`, and it is wrong for the same reason — it would assert a decision to do nothing on a fire that may have just promoted a strategy.
       - **The 22 historical non-NULL rows are NOT retro-relabelled, and the rule forbidding it is explicit.** §Decision-log lifecycle: corrections are NEW rows carrying `superseded_by`, never `UPDATE`/`DELETE`, and "`entry_type` has no sanctioned UPDATE exception (only `sub_pattern` does, ops/RUNBOOK.md §21)" — which binds `decision` identically, since a `sp_log_decision` correction can only ever append. They stand as read-tolerated historical spellings, exactly as `position-close` does for the exit family, and any arsenal-side view built later must allowlist them rather than assume one. **Prose only — no SQL, no view, no threshold, no gate and no declaration is changed, and SL3 owns the surface edited, so no referral is owed for SL3's own leg.**
       - **THE SIBLING LEGS CARRY THE SAME DEFECT AND ARE NOT SL3'S TO FIX — recorded rather than carried silently.** The `entry_type` is three-way shared (pinned 2026-09-07), and across all 57 `arsenal-heartbeat` rows in the current view the non-SL3 ones add **`REJECT` (2, SL1) and `NO RETIREMENT PROPOSED` (1, SL4)** — both coined, and the second not even token-shaped. SL1's STEP 6 and SL4's STEP 4 are the write sites and each owns its own section, so this is raised as an `ops.alerts` `info` row (`heartbeat_decision_vocabulary_drift`) naming both, per the 2026-08-28 OUT-OF-SCOPE FINDINGS rule and the return-path convention; the open-`info`-row de-dup check prescribed 2026-09-14 was run first and no open row covers it. **Do NOT "fix" SL1's or SL4's rows from an SL3 fire**, and do not add a `decision` allowlist to any `scripts/check_*.py` from one either — that is a CI change on landed, green history, the failure CLAUDE.md warns about by name.
       - **ONE METHOD NOTE, because it is the reason this was found on the very run that went looking somewhere else.** This fire ran the block-wide template census the 2026-09-15 finding prescribed — all sixteen executable templates in this block re-derived against live procedure signatures and `INFORMATION_SCHEMA`, fifteen clean, the sixteenth reproducing only the SHADOW `stuck_reason` hazard STEP 4 already pins. **That census could not have found this, by construction: it audits TEMPLATES, and the heartbeat is the one instruction in this STEP with no template to audit** — so the instrument returned "not a defect, simply underspecified", right about the specification and blind to the outcome. What found it was the complementary instrument: a census of the ROWS ACTUALLY WRITTEN — the same method this block already uses for `''` occurrences, for `writes:` cells and for `sp_raise_alert` argument lists, pointed for the first time at a write's own HISTORY instead of at its specification. **A template census tests what a call WOULD do; only the rows test what it DID. And the instruction that executes most often is the one least likely to carry a template, precisely because nothing ever broke.** **Extend the re-resolve once more: each fire, re-measure the referrals, the quotations, the executable templates — and, for every write this STEP performs on EVERY fire, the distinct values its own rows carry in any controlled-vocabulary column.**
